#2941. Federal Reserve Board releases results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families

An official website of the United States Government

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2936. Junk bonds are 'flashing yellow.' Watch these warning signs

Cracks are forming in the junk bond market as investors demand higher payouts for owning the market's riskiest debt. It isn't time to ditch high-yield bonds, but investors should pay attention to the warning signs.

High-yield bonds now yield 8.1%, up from 7.22% a month ago. The increase reflects a jump in yields across the curve as investors bake in more inflation from high energy prices and other pressures, including concern about the deficit — hitting nearly $2 trillion in the fiscal year that ended Sept. 30.

The high-yield market is also showing stress on the credit side with spreads recently widening to levels not seen since April, according to the Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis. Credit spreads are the difference in yield between the bonds and Treasurys of similar maturities. Wider spreads mean investors are demanding higher yields for holding corporate debt, viewing it as riskier.

Spreads are at 315 basis points in the overall high-yield market, higher than a year ago but still below levels in March when they reached 346 bps. One basis point equals one one-hundredth of a percent, or 0.01%.

The high-yield market consists of bonds rated BB+ by S&P and Fitch and Ba1 and under by Moody's. The lowest-rated cohort, CCC and below, has seen the most movement with spreads climbing dramatically over the past year to roughly 1,250 bps.

Right now, the high-yield market is "flashing yellow" but is "far from red," said Michael Arone, chief investment strategist at State Street Investment Management.

It makes sense that investors are demanding more compensation for taking on additional credit risk as borrowing costs rise, he said.

Yields are elevated across the board with the 10-year Treasury reaching its highest level since 2002 earlier in the week.

"The bigger question is whether this is simply a repricing of interest rate risk, or the beginning of a more fundamental reassessment of credit quality," Arone said.

He's in a wait-and-see mode since earnings are still growing, interest-coverage ratios remain good, and while default rates have ticked up some, he believes it is not concerning.

That said, the starting point in spreads is likely weighing on investors' psyche, since they are still low by historical standards.

"There's a small margin of error here, which I also think raises the anxiety level," Arone explained. "The compensation that investors are receiving for taking on this credit risk isn't overwhelming relative to history, and therefore subtle changes in credit spreads can be concerning."

While there may be concerns about part of the lower-rated market, the overall high-yield market is in good fundamental shape.

In fact, credit quality is at a record high, with BB bonds making up over 60% of the market compared with 38% prior to the global financial crisis, said Kelley Gerrity, a fixed income strategist at Morgan Stanley Investment Management.

"We've had higher-quality companies coming in, and with higher rates now, you also have more discipline from companies that are more indebted ... just because of the higher cost of capital, so that actually is creating a bit of a healthier picture as well," she said.

The lowest tier of high yield has always been more speculative, since the bonds have a higher default risk. Therefore, it's no surprise that they are the ones seeing the most widening in spreads, said Collin Martin, head of fixed income research and strategy at the Schwab Center for Financial Research.

The overall move in high-yield spreads have been "orderly," he said. "Are there cracks forming? I think the cracks are logical cracks with the lowest-rated bonds and it's too early to say that it's spreading to the broad credit market."

In addition, fluctuations within the CCC-rated and lower spreads have been idiosyncratic, Gerrity added. Morgan Stanley recently broke down the spreads in the lowest cohort into two buckets: performing assets and non-performing assets, which are defined as spreads above 1,000 bps.

The non-performing spread to worst is 2,818 bps, while the performing bucket — the biggest piece of the CCC market — is 461 bps, she said.

"It is isolated," she said. "I'm not necessarily sure that that will go away anytime soon, but it doesn't tell the story of overall concern and flashing caution for credit markets right now."

For now, investors should remain selective within higher yield, Gerrity advised.

"We're looking for the best relative value opportunities, and we think that exists within that single-B cohort, and so we're leaning into that," she said.

Investors should be concerned if there is a steep widening in the broader high-yield market.

But there's scant evidence of stress in the BB cohort. Spreads in that group are at 194 bps, up from 179 bps a year ago — although the move hasn't been in a straight line.

"We're going to be focusing our attention on what are considered some stronger businesses," Martin said. "If we start to see the markets demand higher spreads there also, that's what we'll be looking at to see if risks are really rising."

It's also important to understand that the Federal Reserve is raising rates in a strong economic environment, said R.J. Gallo, chief investment officer of global fixed income at Federated Hermes. The central bank is concerned about an inflation problem amid high fossil fuel prices due to the Iran war, while growth has been stronger than expected, he noted.

"With growth being good as the reason that the Fed is hiking, then you wouldn't expect high yield to blow out because growth means revenues stay up, means cash flow and profitability stays up," he explained.

High yield becomes a disaster when the economy is heading into a sharp economic downturn, Gallo said.

"That's when spreads really widen out," he said. "But a recession is not the odds-on bet. Now, talk to me in six months if the Fed keeps hiking over and over, if oil prices stay high for longer, well, then maybe we will start to wonder."

#2931. Luka Doncic injury update: What we know after Lakers star rolls ankle late in win vs Bucks

The Los Angeles Lakers' blowout over the Milwaukee Bucks on Wednesday, Nov. 11 may have come at a steep cost.

Austin Reaves exited the game in the third quarter and did not return with a left heel injury. Collin Sexton later went into the locker room himself after taking a headbutt in the fourth, and then Luka Dončić appeared to roll his right ankle with just over four minutes to go.

Dončić hit a stepback 3 (later overturned to a long 2-pointer) over Kyle Kuzma, but his ankle twisted as soon as it landed on the ground and sent the two-time scoring champion crashing to the floor, where he grabbed at his leg and scooted over to the scorer's table to take a minute.

Dončić was able to get up and walk around, and made a free throw to convert the and-1 before head coach JJ Redick mass subbed the entire lineup with the Lakers up 24 points at the 4:25 mark. He finished the game with 32 points, five rebounds and five assists, continuing his red-hot start to the season.

Redick told reporters postgame that he believes Dončić appears to be fine. Dončić echoed those sentiments after the game.

"I'm good, obviously it's painful a little bit but I'll be fine," Dončić said.

He wouldn't have even been in the game at that point, Redick added, had Sexton not bumped his head. Sexton received stitches in his temple area and X-Rays on Reaves came back negative, though he will undergo further testing on Thursday.

They'll all have more time to recover than they've been used to; the Lakers have three days off for the first time this season before finishing their four-game road trip on Sunday against the Phoenix Suns.

"It does feel long, I got to be honest with you," Redick told reporters. "It's three games in four nights, and we played Portland on Friday in the Cup game at home, and that feels like it was three weeks ago."

This story has been updated with new information.

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2924. Lakers rout Bucks for seventh straight win

The Lakers used a big run in the third quarter to blow out the Bucks on Wednesday, 123-112.

LA led for most of the game, but the Bucks remained feisty for much of the contest. It wasn't until the third quarter when the Lakers blew the game open and fully took control.

Milwaukee showed how woeful they are, turning the ball over 19 times, leading to 28 points. LA's bench was strong again, scoring 55 points.

The concern coming out of the game was a variety of injuries. Austin Reaves left in the third quarter with a heel injury and Collin Sexton took a blow to the face. Luka Dončić twisted his ankle after a hard closeout and left the game after shooting the subsequent free throw.

Brayden Burries gave the Bucks an early lead, but Luka and Austin started to cook, scoring a combined 14 points to put Los Angeles ahead. The Lakers were on a 12-0 scoring run.

Luka continued his dominant play and, as we are used to seeing every game, was already in double figures with 14 points.

Kyle Kuzma and Tyler Herro added 12 points to help keep Milwaukee close. The Lakers were up six with 3:27 left.

LA allowed Jaime Jacquez Jr. to start heating up. He scored eight points that cut Los Angeles' lead down to two at the end of the first.

Walker Kessler got on the board early in the second period with a layup for LA. Sandro Mamukelashvili picked up right where he left off last game, providing an excellent spark off the bench with five points. Collin Sexton also pitched in with four points.

The Bucks were sticking around, though, cutting a 10-point Lakers lead to four, led by Jacquez Jr and Herro.

Milwaukee was playing scrappy and keeping pace with LA. The Bucks were shooting 50% from the field and Los Angeles was at 42%.

The Lakers offense woke back up as they went on a quick 7-0 run on the shoulders of Sexton and Luka.

Milwaukee was on a three-minute scoring drought until Herro scored. It seemed the Bucks were inching closer again, but Mamu and Reaves combined for nine points to help give the purple and gold an 11-point lead at halftime.

Reaves drained a triple to start the third period for Los Angeles. Luka also knocked down one of his own. Burries scored on a triple for the Bucks. LA kept their double-digit lead, despite shooting 3-9 in the quarter.

Los Angeles then blew the game open, extending its lead to 17 as Thybulle and Kessler combined for five points.

The concerning moment came in the middle of the middle of the quarter when Austin suffered his heel injury on his landing after attempting a dunk. He left shortly after and did not return.

The rest of the quarter was Mamu continuing his incredible play off the bench, pushing his point total to 21 for the night. Luka ended the third by converting on a three-point play that gave the purple and gold a 21-point lead.

LA maintained their big lead well into the final frame. Mamu, Kessler, Williams and Sexton each contributed offensively. Herro and Jacquez hadn't scored a field goal, but converted on six free throws.

Sexton suffered his injury in the final frame, taking a shot to the face while on defense and being forced to leave with an apparent cut.

Luka had scored six points before being subbed out after appearing to injure his ankle. The Lakers then subbed in their bench at the 4:25 mark.

Luka finished with 32 points, five rebounds, five assists and three steals. Reaves ended with 18 points and five assists before leaving with an injury. Kessler logged 10 points with 11 rebounds and three assists.

Mamu pitched in with 23 points off the bench. Sexton had 10 points. Thybulle scored eight points with five rebounds. Ziaire Williams and Jarred Vanderbilt combined for 13 points and six assists.

The Lakers' next game will be against the Phoenix Suns on Sunday at 1:00 PM PT.

#2916. Austin Reaves injured as Lakers win seventh in a row

The Lakers won their seventh straight game. They could lose something bigger.

Star guard Austin Reaves left Wednesday's 123-112 win over the Milwaukee Bucks with a left heel injury and did not return.

Reaves, who scored 18 points with five assists and three steals, landed awkwardly on a blocked dunk attempt during the third quarter but stayed in the game despite appearing to be in discomfort on the bench during an ensuing timeout. He remained in the game for three minutes before appearing to aggravate the injury on a drive toward the basket. He asked to sub himself out on the next possession.

Reaves' initial X-rays were negative, Lakers coach JJ Redick said, and the guard, averaging a career-best 23.5 points on 50.8% shooting, will undergo further imaging. Initial tests have ruled out any potential Achilles injury, Redick said.

The Lakers finished their first extended trip of the season unscathed in the standings, improving to 11-2 and still the best record in the league, but had to limp across the finish line Wednesday after injuries struck.

Guard Collin Sexton received an eyebrow's worth of stitches over his right eye after knocking heads with Milwaukee Bucks guard Ryan Rollins with 6:51 remaining in the fourth quarter. Sexton's sudden departure forced Luka Doncic to re-enter a 20-point game.

Two-and-a-half minutes later, Doncic turned his ankle while getting fouled by Kyle Kuzma on a long two-point jumper.

Doncic, who led the Lakers with 32 points, five assists, five rebounds and three steals, grabbed at his right ankle immediately. Lakers staff members tended to him while Kuzma's foul was reviewed for a potential flagrant, but Doncic stayed in the game to shoot the free throw. He got subbed out right after.

Doncic said after the game he felt fine. Now he just has to wait for news on his backcourt running mate.

Read more: 'It's forever': Lakers rookie Cameron Carr cherishing first NBA points

"We just gotta have next-man-up mentality," Doncic said of the potential of playing without Reaves. "Obviously we lose a very, very good player. Hopefully ... it's nothing. We gotta have next-man-up mentality."

Reaves just signed a maximum contract extension this summer and was off to a career year. He is the 15th-leading scorer in the NBA, and he and Doncic (38.6 points per game) form the highest-scoring pair of teammates in the league. Reaves is also averaging 3.4 rebounds and 4.6 assists while shooting a career-best 41.3% from three-point range.

Reaves, who played almost an entire game on what turned into a Grade 2 oblique strain suffered last April against Oklahoma City, had missed the Lakers' previous game against the Atlanta Hawks because of illness.

Guard Quentin Grimes missed his second consecutive game Wednesday with a sprained finger on his right hand. Grimes was questionable for Wednesday's game, but still had swelling, Redick said, which could have affected the right-handed guard's shooting.

Read more: What we've learned about the first-place Lakers

Forward Sandro Mamukelashvili delivered for the shorthanded Lakers both nights, scoring 23 points of the bench for the second consecutive game Wednesday. The Georgian forward went 11 for 35 from three-point range during the first 11 games of the season and suddenly found his shooting stroke with consecutive five-of-eight nights against the Hawks and Bucks.

Mamukelashvili helped lift the Lakers through a five-day stretch that Redick said felt more like three weeks. Last Friday, the Lakers played the Portland Trail Blazers at home in an NBA Cup game and have since climbed to the top of the NBA standings, won two games against teams in the top half of the Eastern Conference and watched both of their star guards leave a game with injuries.

"We're all ready to go home," said center Walker Kessler, who had 10 points and 11 rebounds for his seventh double-double in eight games. "I'll just say that."

Battered and bruised from a tiring rip, the Lakers return home for a brief respite. The Lakers will have multiple days of rest before their next game Sunday at Phoenix. They don't face a team with a winning record until Nov. 27. The road game in San Antonio figures to be a marquee showdown between Western Conference title contenders.

#2914. American soldier arrested on suspicion of spying for Russia

A senior soldier in the U.S. Army has been arrested in the U.K. on suspicion of spying for Russia, London's Metropolitan Police said on Tuesday evening.

The unnamed 41-year-old man was arrested near Gloucester, a city in western England, on Tuesday as part of an investigation by the U.K.'s Counter Terrorism Policing. British police worked with U.S. authorities, including the military and the FBI, in their investigations.

A senior NATO military official confirmed on Tuesday that the American service member was assigned to the military alliance's Allied Rapid Reaction Corps (ARRC).

ARRC, headquartered in the English county of Gloucestershire, is a  3-star NATO headquarters with more than 400 permanent staff from more than 20 nations ready to deploy land forces where they are needed.

Kremlin spokesman Dmitry Peskov told reporters on Wednesday that Moscow had "absolutely nothing to do with" the case in the U.K.

The individual at the center of the case, detained in London, is suspected of committing offenses under Sections 1 and 3 of the U.K.'s National Security Act.

Section 1 offenses relate to obtaining or disclosing protected information, while Section 3 involves assisting a foreign intelligence service.

The maximum penalty is life imprisonment for a Section 1 offense and 14 years' imprisonment for a Section 3 offense. A fine may also be imposed for either offense.

Police officers are carrying out searches at two locations in Gloucestershire, the Metropolitan Police added.

The arrest comes as Washington is seeking to broker an end to Russia's war in Ukraine. U.S.-mediated talks between Russian and Ukrainian officials began in Abu Dhabi on Tuesday, the first such meeting since February, while Kyiv and its European allies continue to press for credible security guarantees as part of any settlement.

President Donald Trump, a longstanding critic of NATO and its members' defense budgets, said on Monday that there appeared to be a growing willingness on both sides to end the almost five-year war.

Russia has long threatened retaliatory action against an expansion of NATO's eastern flank and has previously demanded guarantees that Ukraine will never be allowed to join the alliance. Finland and Sweden became NATO's newest members in 2023 and 2024, respectively.

Since Russia and Ukraine have been at war, Russian activity has also been reported deeper into European and NATO territory, with regional authorities investigating whether Moscow could be involved in some of the recent disarray seen across the continent.

FBI Director Kash Patel said in a statement on Tuesday evening that the allegations against a U.S. serviceman were "of the utmost concern to the United States and our allies."

"I want to send a clear message – this FBI has no tolerance for anyone who breaks their oath to the United States and whose actions endanger our national security and that of our partners," he said.

Sean Stinchon, the commanding general of U.S. Army Counterintelligence Command, said in a statement that "the alleged conduct of a senior solider, entrusted with sensitive information, is a sobering reminder that insider threats are real and persistent."

Authorities said Tuesday that the investigation is not related to any other ongoing Counter Terrorism Policing investigations.

On Oct. 27, British prosecutors said they had charged a man with assisting a foreign intelligence service over an incident at a military base used by the U.S. for strikes on Iran. The base in question, RAF Fairford, is in Gloucestershire, around 30 miles from ARRC's headquarters at Imjin Barracks.

#2910. Zelenskyy warns Ukraine is 'not real estate' as experts say a Russia peace deal remains elusive

Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy warned that his country "was not real estate," as a second and final day of U.S.-brokered peace talks wrapped up.

Speaking to reporters in Denmark late Tuesday, Zelenskyy said he was "happy" with what he was hearing from the peace talks in Abu Dhabi between Russian and Ukrainian representatives, but said he had been clear with U.S. envoys Steve Witkoff and Jared Kushner that "this is a country of our nation, it is not real estate."

His comments come amid discussions on the future borders of Ukraine and post-war security guarantees, issues where the countries remain far apart.

"Everybody is ready for dialog. First, stop the war in Ukraine. Second, security guarantees for Ukraine. Third, maybe some dialog about these topics of security," Zelenskyy said. "This sequence is very, very important."

It comes as the first trilateral peace talks between Russia, Ukraine and the U.S. since February concluded on Wednesday.

Speaking post-meeting, Kushner called the talks "productive" and said the parties had also discussed the difficult issue of territorial boundaries. The Ukrainian negotiator meanwhile said momentum was good and a solution could be reached before the end of the year.

Russia chief negotiator and Putin aide Vladimir Medinsky said the parties on Wednesday had reached an agreement on a large-scale prisoner exchange, and that details would be announced soon.

Another round of exchanges of prisoners of war would be welcomed, Mykola Bielieskov, senior analyst at Ukrainian NGO Come Back Alive, told CNBC Tuesday. "Otherwise there is no room for compromise," he said, adding, "I don't have any expectations of major progress towards settlement."

According to Bielieskov, Ukraine could accept a ceasefire along the current front lines, but would never formally recognize Russian sovereignty over occupied territory. He argued that Zelenskyy has neither the legal authority nor public support to surrender Ukrainian land, even if Kyiv acknowledges it cannot reclaim those territories militarily in the near term.

Russia's demands for additional territory, restrictions on NATO membership and limits on Ukraine's armed forces remain fundamental obstacles to a deal, he added.

Russia has publicly maintained its demand that Ukraine surrender the entire eastern Donbas region, including territory Moscow has not captured.

While Ukraine and European allies push for a ceasefire as an initial step, before moving on to issues such as security guarantees, borders and reconstruction, Russia has signaled it considers security guarantees necessary before ending fighting.

"It looks like because Russia has objected to a ceasefire proposal that Ukraine and the U.S. earlier last year made, the Trump administration is no longer trying to achieve a ceasefire, even though that's logically the first step," former U.S. ambassador William Courtney told CNBC early Tuesday.

Courtney said Russia has shown no signs of seriously pursuing a ceasefire in Ukraine, despite Kyiv's willingness to freeze the conflict along current battle lines.

He argued that Moscow's territorial demands and continued attacks suggest its objectives remain unchanged, making meaningful negotiations unlikely without a shift in Russia's position or battlefield conditions.

Maximilian Hess at political risk firm Enmetena Advisory told CNBC's Dan Murphy Wednesday, ahead of the second day of talks, that little has been achieved in the latest round of meetings so far.

While Zelenskyy has urged leaders to speak directly, Hess said it's unlikely the Ukrainian leader will be willing to meet with Putin without meaningful concessions from Russia, given the risk of domestic backlash.

He argued that Putin could use a potential meeting with Trump and Xi Jinping in China to seek further U.S. sanctions relief, weaken Washington's support for Ukraine, and deepen divisions within the West.

On a potential Zelenskyy-Putin meeting, Kushner said the Russian position is that "they'd like to see more progress made before that happens".

#2909. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Uncertainty in the Middle East, elevated Treasury yields and fears about an AI bubble continue to keep global markets on edge. Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking stable income can consider enhancing their portfolios by adding dividend-paying stocks.

However, the vast universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Tracking the ratings of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as their recommendations are backed by in-depth research.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Chord Energy is an independent exploration and production company with long-lived assets primarily in the Williston Basin. With a quarterly base dividend of $1.30 per share, CHRD offers a dividend yield of 3.67%.

In a research note on third-quarter expectations, RBC Capital analyst Scott Hanold reiterated a buy rating on CHRD stock with a price target of $175. "We model production near the top of guidance and continue to see strong performance from new wells and base production," said Hanold.

Specifically, the five-star analyst expects Q3 2026 production at 283 thousand barrels of oil equivalent per day, or Mboe/d, in line with the Street's consensus and near the high end of the 278 to 284 Mboe/d guidance. Hanold said his Q3 2026 earnings per share estimate increased to $5.29 due to lower non-cash depreciation, depletion and amortization.

Meanwhile, cash flow per share estimate moved lower to $13.59 to reflect Hanold's update based on the current backdrop and commodity mark-to-market. He expects Chord Energy to report capital spending of $375 million for Q3 2026, slightly above the Street's estimate of $369 million.

Overall, Hanold remains bullish on CHRD, given its best-in-class balance sheet. He expects a free cash flow payout of 80% in the third quarter, with the possibility of an upside. Hanold expects investors to focus on management commentary on 2027 capital plans, Williston M&A opportunities, and long-term performance of 4-mile wells.

Hanold ranks No. 310 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 14.9%. See Chord Energy KPIs on TipRanks.

Williams, an energy infrastructure company that delivers natural gas, is the next dividend pick. The company recently completed the $5.5 billion acquisition of Momentum Midstream, which will expand its natural gas infrastructure in the Haynesville to meet growing Gulf Coast LNG, power and industrial demand. At a quarterly dividend of $0.5250 per share, Williams offers a yield of 2.89%.

In her latest research note, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $87. Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, the analyst said that she expects WMB to announce strong quarterly performance.

Scotto raised her estimates to reflect insights from the quarterly catch-up call, the completion of the Momentum Midstream acquisition, and mark-to-market quarter-to-date commodity prices. She expects WMB to report Q3 2026 adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization of $2,036 million, which is modestly above the Street's consensus estimate.

Furthermore, Scotto expects Williams' Transmission, Power & Gulf segment to increase quarter-over-quarter, driven by the startup of the Socrates power project and favorable natural gas liquids, or NGL, margins.

Scotto noted that WMB stock has underperformed due to backlash related to data centers and investor anticipation of another Power Innovation project. She expects the company to announce at least one more project before the end of this year, potentially with a new customer and a contract duration of 15 to 20 years, which would be a positive catalyst for the stock.

Scotto ranks No. 175 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been profitable 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.2%. See Williams Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

EOG Resources, a crude oil and natural gas exploration and production company, will hold its third-quarter earnings call on Nov. 6. At a quarterly dividend of $1.02 per share, EOG offers a dividend yield of 2.75%.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Jefferies analyst Lloyd Byrne reiterated a buy rating on EOG Resources stock and increased his price target to $185 from $175. The analyst expects the company's Q3 cash flow per share to exceed the Street's consensus by 13%, driven by better-than-expected production.

Byrne expects crude oil production, excluding the UAE, to reach 551 Mboe/d, which is at the high end of guidance (546-551 Mboe/d) and above the Street's projection of 548 Mboe/d, driven by continued growth in the Utica.

Furthermore, Byrne expects cash operating expenses to come in at $10.27 per barrel of oil equivalent, below the midpoint of guidance of $10.05 to $11.35, helped by lower lease operating expenses. Overall, the analyst expects EOG to deliver free cash flow of $2.65 billion, supporting another quarter of buybacks near the minimum 70% shareholder return threshold and implying about $1.5 billion in share repurchases.

"As a first indication of the '27 plan, we expect EOG will message another year of mid-single-digit (~3.5%) growth on a flat capital budget (~$6.55bn), which is above the Street modeling 1% oil growth at $6.6bn," said Byrne.

Byrne ranks No. 303 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 18.5%. See EOG Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

#2908. Nets blow past Pelicans with an offensive resurgence, win 145-111

After their hardest hitting loss of the season, the Big Easy was the perfect place for the Brooklyn Nets to go next on their road trip. The Nets obliterated the New Orleans Pelicans this evening at the Smoothie King Center. Few wins have been more necessary, and even fewer more deserved.

However, the contest wasn't advertised as the breakaway layup it turned into.

The Pelicans were at home, and with a Zion Williamson coming off three days of rest. Meanwhile, Brooklyn's young guards had just taken a spin on the injury carousel. Before the game, the Nets announced that Egor Dëmin, who hasn't played in two weeks due to a bone bruise, would return tonight. However, the team also said that Nolan Traoré, who hadn't missed a game yet, would sit with right calf soreness. They also ruled out Mikel Brown Jr. due to an illness.

So, ball-handling was at a premium in the bayou. Dëmin and Danny Wolf made up the starting back court, with usual suspects Michael Porter Jr., Julius Randle, and Day'Ron Sharpe filling in behind them. Call it big ball. Indeed, it may have the first starting line-up ever featuring five players 6'9" or taller.

With the aforementioned injuries, Jordi Fernández didn't have many choices when putting together his starting five, but still made the right ones. With the Pelicans giving up more opponent points in the paint than anyone so far this year, beginning with the jumbo lineup made sense, and it showed on the court, with the Nets outscoring New Orleans 24-10 inside during the first period. The most they had in an opening frame all season before that was 18.

Evidenced by the above, Brooklyn's reserves got in on the action all the same. Noah Clowney, fresh off a game where he fouled out in just over seven minutes of play, knew he had to take advantage and acted accordingly, putting up six points with a block and a steal in the opening period. Wagner also added seven points, Wolf had five, and Porter Jr. led with nine. The Nets also led after one, up 39-25, giving them their largest advantage after a first quarter since April 2024.

Effective and easy-to-follow, Brooklyn had no reason to abandon its game plan in the subsequent period. The Nets got eight of their first 11 points in the second inside, and after MPJ topped that off with a three from the top of the key, Brooklyn had gone up by 17 with 7:29 to go in the second.

Holding it down from deep while everyone else went to work inside, that wasn't even Porter Jr.' best hit from downtown. With a little under four to go in the first, he also dethroned Derik Queen with this diagonal step-back…

And, knowing full well how harmful poor paint defense can be, the Nets were careful not to give up what their opponents had so frivolously surrendered. Sharpe was especially quick tonight fading back to provide extra defense around the rim, even as Zion Williamson continued to try and rummage his way in. New Orleans scored only twice inside in the second.

Saddiq Bey did threaten Brooklyn with another ugly end to a quarter. Hustling down after a Porter Jr. turnover offered up spacing in transition, Bey netted a triple that quickly brought it back to an 11 point game with roughly two and a half minutes left in the half.

The Nets didn't take it lightly. They responded with a 13-7 jolt that involved two threes from Terance Mann, one as a direct response to Bey's and another just before the horn. Brooklyn ended up going into the half up 83-66. It was the most points the Nets had scored a first half since putting up 91 on the Golden State Warriors in 2022. It's the third most points they've ever put up in a first half in team history.

By that point, Brooklyn's paint scoring advantage had swelled 40-24. They also carried a +9 advantage on the boards, along with five rejections.

So, after a period of complete interior dominance, that meant one thing one: it was time for Day'Ron Sharpe to begin shooting threes. No, I'm not kidding. And no, it didn't cost them the game. In fact, it had an opposite effect.

The Brooklyn big man hit two triples in the third's opening five minutes to give his team's lead a quick buff once play resumed. Sharpe also dished one to Egor Dëmin, who was only starting to find his groove at that point, but more on that later.

The Nets weren't just scoring from inside anymore. They were scoring from everywhere, and the Pelicans couldn't do so from anywhere, everywhere, all at once. Not before long, the home team had a look on their face Brooklyn knows all too well: one of discouragement. They stopped boxing out, closing out, and just seemed like they wanted out. The Pelicans only put up 15 points in the period.

And the Nets? Like Bill Murray in the mid 2000s, they were eager to play a new, more serious role. After Noah Clowney scooped and slammed a missed three from Mann, Brooklyn earned their largest lead of the game, going up 104-77 with about three and a half left in the third.

"He impacted the game," Fernández said of Clowney, who he said had things to "figure out" after the Dallas game. "You have size in him. He can guard multiple positions. He's switchable. He's been rebounding very well. And the efficiency, the taking good shots to get to the free throw line. Very happy with him, and we just got to keep taking positive steps here."

In the fourth, it got to a point where you almost wanted the Nets to cut it out, irrationally afraid they were burning through all their makes before the end of the road trip. Keon Ellis and Wagner added back-to-back threes to begin the final frame, getting the team to a 16-34 mark from deep by that point. They'd finish 20-41 from beyond the arc.

The Nets have long used fourth quarter blowouts to give their young players real game reps. Tonight, they got to do so from the other side of the scoreboard. Not long after the Ellis and Wagner threes, Dëmin added his own pair of triples, looking more comfortable on the floor than he has all season. The Nets seemed to run a number of the actions they normally reserve for MPJ, asking him to duck and dive around screens off ball before rising up for a catch-and-shoot three. Like his mentor, he did so each time with confidence…

Coming in like Mariano Rivera in a game where the Yankees were already up seven or eight runs, Dëmin added 12 points in the fourth to bring his total for the game to 22 on 5-of-8 shooting from the field and 4-of-6 shooting from deep.

"What impressed me? Nothing," Dëmin said of the team's overall performance. "I think that's what we're working on every day, and it's not a surprise for me how you we could sustain the energy and effort throughout the whole game, which we really need to do from night to night. Obviously it's hard to do, but it's nothing new about what we're trying to accomplish every single game."

Porter Jr. also finished with 22 points while shooting 7-of-14 from the field and 4-10 from three. He also dished three assists, grabbed a steal, and garnered six rebounds. Randle had 17 points, five assists, and four rebounds after shooting 6-of-16 from the field. Sharpe had 15 points, five assists, four rebounds, and four blocks, shooting 5-of-6 from the field and 2-of-3 from deep.

Off the bench, both Wagner and Clowney had 16 points each. Wagner added four assists while shooting 6-of=8 from the field. Clowney shot 5-of-7 from the field and 2-of-3 from three, also grabbing two steals, a block, and three rebounds. Ellis had 11 points, four rebounds, three assists, and a steal. Wolf reached 10 points, 11 rebounds, four assists, and two blocks.

"We've been practicing very well and working very well, and it's good to see that you play how you practice," Fernández said. "So, happy for the group, proud of the guys. I mean, you see everybody. Mike was composed the whole time and talked to his teammates. Julius is +38 in 29 minutes. Egor huge minutes. Mo scoring. Terrence two huge winning plays. Everybody was a big part of the win."

"Just getting into our actions, getting organized, and just having some structure," Randle added. "We were all on the same page, and we've been working at it, so it's good to see."

Final: Brooklyn Nets 145, New Orleans Pelicans 111

Day'Ron Sharpe scored 15 points with four rebounds, five assists and a career-high-tying four blocks tonight in New Orleans. It is his fourth consecutive game with at least five assists, which establishes a franchise-record streak of games with 5+ AST by a center.

Julius Randle recorded a +38 plus/minus tonight in New Orleans, which tied Stephon Marbury (4/16/99 at Seattle) as the highest plus/minus by a Net in a road game in franchise history. It tied the seventh-highest in any game by a Net.

Danny Wolf recorded a career-high 11 rebounds tonight in New Orleans to go with 10 points, four assists and two blocks. It is his second double-double of the season and fourth of his career.

Six Nets scored 15+ PTS tonight at NOP: Porter Jr., Randle, Sharpe, Dëmin, Wagner and Clowney. It ties the franchise record (15th instance) and is their second such game of the season (10/27 at BOS). This is the fourth season in franchise history with multiple such games.

Brooklyn's 83 first-half points tonight vs. New Orleans were the team's most in a first half since scoring 91 on 12/21/2022 vs. Golden State. Their 39 points in the first quarter tonight at New Orleans were their most in an opening frame this season and second most in the last two seasons, trailing only their 46 points on 2/7/26 vs. Washington.

This morning, veteran NBA writer James Herbert published a story for The Ringer on our very own Net Income. In it, you can hear from Pooch, Lucas, Sean Marks, and the countless others who've gotten close with the man behind Nets Daily over the years. I encourage all of you to check it out, and throw James a follow, who is as talented of a story teller as you'll find.

Brooklyn concludes its roadtrip on Saturday evening with a bout against the Memphis Grizzlies in Grind City. At the time of writing, the Grizz are 7-3. Isn't it cool when the lottery decides to bless you instead flip you upside down, tar and feather you? This one tips off at 8:00 p.m. EST.

#2906. How Democrats could snarl Trump administration stakes in private sector companies

With Republicans in charge of the White House and Congress, President Donald Trump has embedded the federal government in private sector companies' affairs in unprecedented ways. That could change once Trump's Democratic adversaries take over the House and Senate following the blue wave midterm.

Democrats, planning to root out alleged corruption involving Trump, his family and their business associates, have signaled they will closely scrutinize companies that have curried the president's favor.

Their investigations may extend to Intel and the dozens of other companies that have clinched equity stakes with the government during Trump's second term. Democratic lawmakers in July asked law firm Skadden Arps for details about its involvement in the Intel deal.

The dynamic could affect how Trump approaches relationships with private sector companies, after spending two years involving the government in their affairs through stock agreements or, in the case of U.S. Steel, acquiring a "golden share" when the company was acquired by Nippon Steel.

The golden share, which allows a holder to outvote other shareholders, was negotiated as part of the June 2025 deal. The highly unusual arrangement gives Trump veto power over several key areas of the steelmaker's business, including those related to employee salaries, capital investment and even the company name.

In a unique twist, the company's amended charter specifies that after Trump leaves office, the veto power passes not to his successor, but to the Treasury and Commerce departments as representatives of the U.S. government.

U.S. Steel CEO David Burritt said Wednesday that the government's representative on its board, Commerce Department official David Shapiro, typically abstains from voting but has access to company discussions.

"He gets to hear everything we're doing," Burritt told CNBC's Megan Cassella at the U.S. Chamber of Commerce's B20 summit in Washington, adding that the arrangement has not constrained the company's operations.

"The government has not stepped in and said 'you can't do this or can't do that,' but we know what the rules are," Burritt said.

The U.S. Steel intervention turned out to be just the beginning of the U.S. government taking stakes in companies, a highly unusual move stateside. A month after acquiring the golden share, the Trump administration agreed to a $400 million stock deal with rare earths company MP Materials. The month after that, Trump announced the U.S. would take a 10% stake in chipmaker Intel.

By January, one year into Trump's second term as president, the administration had established some form of equity position in 14 companies, according to the CATO Institute's tracker. That number skyrocketed to 41 by November, including nine such announcements in May alone, the tracker shows. The holdings include both publicly traded and privately held companies, and CATO tracks deals that are closed as well as others that have been made public but are still being negotiated.

Following Republicans' decisive defeat in the Nov. 3 midterm election, the federal government has not revealed any new stock positions. But Trump, entering lame-duck status and on the verge of losing political power over Congress, could keep trying to steer his government to forge more equity deals with companies, said Indiana University associate professor Sarah Bauerle Danzman, who researches the intersection of national security and investment policy.

"He might feel he has less to lose," Bauerle Danzman, who is also a senior fellow at the Atlantic Council, told CNBC.

White House spokesman Matthew Middleton told CNBC in a statement, "Through voluntary equity investments in willing companies, the Trump Administration has partnered with the private sector to rebuild America's industrial base and bring critical production back home.""Moving forward, the President is willing to work with anyone to advance solutions that strengthen our economy and put more money back in the pockets of American workers," Middleton said.

But Trump's so-far unimpeded efforts may face sudden resistance once Democrats take majority control of the House and Senate in January.

Democratic lawmakers are openly gearing up to score wins against Trump and his associates as they vow to crack down on alleged rampant government corruption.

Those probes are expected to center on tech giants like Apple, Meta Platforms and Elon Musk's companies, whose executives have spent millions cozying up to the administration. Democrats are also poised to dig into potential conflicts related to the bevy of critical minerals deals Trump has struck. Lawmakers of both parties are also looking at Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang, the close Trump ally engulfed in scandal after admitting an extramarital affair with a suspected Chinese spy.

The administration's equity interventions could become a target in that mission, said Bauerle Danzman, predicting that Democrats might seek to boost oversight efforts even if they don't try to cancel the existing stock arrangements.

The next Congress could try to ensure the government is taking stakes that are "forwarding genuine national security priorities" and that do not provide "special favors to political and family connections," she said.

"The story there is more about kind of tying these issues to ones of accountability and anti-corruption, as opposed to necessarily cost," Bauerle Danzman said.

Asked whether U.S. Steel was preparing for potential congressional investigations after Democrats take control, Burritt said he was unaware of any investigation but would cooperate if one materialized.

"I am not aware that they're investigating, but if they are, we're happy to work with them," Burritt said. "It's not a big deal for us because we know we did the right thing."

It's not clear how strongly Republicans would try to defend Trump's golden share or his government's equity stakes should they become a Democratic target.

Conservatives traditionally espouse free markets and denounce excessive government meddling in the private sector. Republicans, including Trump, spent much of the midterm campaign warning that Democrats aim to destroy the U.S. capitalist system and impose economy-killing big government socialism.

Some Republicans have criticized the Trump administration's corporate interventions, but the moves have received little pushback from the president's party overall.

But in the wake of a crushing electoral defeat, some Republicans "might feel emboldened and actually join with the Democrats" in trying to clamp down on Trump, Bauerle Danzman said.

Burritt, despite embracing the government's involvement in U.S. Steel, said corporate leaders should retain responsibility for running their businesses.

"We need to keep accountability at the right place. We need to keep it with, with the leaders of companies and with the companies individually," he said. "We need to be capitalists."

#2902. Charles Barkley sounds off on epic rant after comparisons of Austin Reaves to Larry Bird: ‘They’re both white. That’s it.’

Charles Barkley admits he loves Austin Reaves, but made it clear to never again compare him to NBA legend, Larry Bird.

On Wednesday night, the Hall of Famer went off on an expletive-filled rant after he saw a clip from the Pistons broadcast team, comparing Reaves to Bird after he made an impressive shot during the Lakers' 121-116 victory on Sunday night.

"I was watching the Detroit Pistons against the Lakers the other night, and some idiot, or some fool, or jackass, thought it would be appropriate to compare Austin Reaves to Larry Bird," Barkley said. "Y'all put together a team and said, 'Who does Austin Reaves remind me of?' and y'all put together a video clip of Austin Reaves."

Charles Barkley sounds off on Pistons broadcaster who compared Austin Reaves to Larry Bird on Sunday night during "Inside the NBA." X / @ESPNNBA

"I love Austin Reaves. Don't compare him to Larry Bird ever. Under no damn circumstances."

Barkley doubled down on his take and said he loves Reaves, but "Do not ever do that again."

"Yeah, they're both white. That's it. That's the only thing they have in common. Nothing else," Barkley said.

Austin Reaves celebrating after putting together a 43-point performance against the Pistons on Sunday night. Getty Images

While Barkley revealed his true feelings for Reaves, the Lakers guard was the star on Sunday.

The team has grown accustomed to Luka Dončić scoring 40+ points in a game, but as the Lakers were without the early MVP candidate, Reaves stole the spotlight.

Aside from yelling at a Pistons fan's face, Reaves put together a 43-point performance, where he was 4-for-6 from the three-point line, shot 51.9% from the field, and 11-for-11 from the free-throw line.

Austin Reaves notches a layup in Sunday's game against the Pistons. Getty Images

Aside from scoring in abundance, Reaves also had five rebounds and four assists.

Reaves is starting to develop into the player the Lakers knew he could become, and is starting to look like he was worth every penny the team gave him after he signed a four-year, $185 million contract during the offseason.

#2901. Giants news: Game preview, Arvell Reese named Defensive Player Of The Week, more

Good morning, New York Giants fans! Here are the stories you need as the Giants get set to take on their NFC East rivals Washington Commanders tonight.

New York Giants. It is not like things were expected to be much better in New York. Again, looking back at our preseason ranking of all 32 teams, we had the New York Giants in the second-lowest tier, the "Works in Progress" group. But if the NFC playoffs began right now, the Giants would be at home during Wild Card Weekend as the winners of the NFC East, thanks to their 5-2-1 record.

That is surprising enough, but when you consider that New York lost starting quarterback Jaxson Dart early in the year due to an injury, and running back Cam Skattebo has been iced out — he was inactive for New York's Week 9 win over the Philadelphia Eagles — the Giants are doing this without two players we all expected to be cornerstones of their offense. Things will get tougher for New York over the second half of the season. The Giants have games against Jacksonville, San Francisco, and Seattle on the docket, and the Week 17 meeting with the Dallas Cowboys could decide the division. Still, these Giants are one of the biggest surprises in the league at the midway point.

Until they can start winning by wider margins, they will do whatever it takes.

"The bottom line is finding a way to win," Harbaugh said. "It's all or nothing basically from that perspective. You can win by a bunch, you can win by 40, and it's still just one, 1-0. But you'd rather win by 40. There's no doubt about it. Really, [it's] just chasing that, playing better in a way that makes it really tough for your opponent to even stay close if you're good enough to do that. So, we would like to get to that place."

Cam Skattebo walked to the center of the New York Giants' locker room, leaned against the ping-pong table and greeted the small group of reporters converging on his space. "What up?" Skattebo yelped.

A lot's been up with the Giants: trade talk for Jonathan Taylor, which ended with a whimper when the NFL deadline passed roughly 40 minutes after Skattebo's post-practice interview.

In the end, the Giants didn't add a weapon — like running back Jonathan Taylor — at Tuesday's trade deadline. So what now for their offense, as it tries to find stability with replacement quarterback J.J. McCarthy, amid the push for a shocking NFC East title?

"We're still finding our way," said wide receiver Darnell Mooney of the Giants' offense. "What we need to continue to do is find our offense, find our identity. We're still looking for it. We're still looking for what we are great at. I don't think anything has been great [enough] to be like: 'Oh, we have enough.'

7. Malik Nabers, New York Giants (up 1). Nabers is coming off his second-lowest-graded game of the season but moves up one spot with Mike Evans dropping out due to time missed. Being held to 43 yards against the Philadelphia Eagles broke a streak of four straight games with at least 100 receiving yards.

Linebacker. First team: Arvell Reese, New York Giants; Jacob Rodriguez, Miami Dolphins. Reese terrorized the Eagles this past Sunday, to the tune of 12 tackles, three sacks and a forced fumble on a sack that took just 2.29 seconds — the fastest sack in the NFL since Week 7 of last season, according to NextGenStats. Reese was my top-ranked player in the 2026 draft, and he hasn't disappointed. His main appeal as a prospect was his versatility, which is also why he is one of the favorites for Defensive Rookie of the Year. He is the only NFL player (not just among rookies) with 65-plus tackles, eight-plus tackles for loss and at least one interception. Add in his forced fumble from Sunday and 4.5 sacks, and Reese has made an impact for the NFC East-leading Giants.

Interior offensive line. First team: Vega Ioane, Baltimore Ravens; Austin Barber, Cleveland Browns; Francis Mauigoa, New York Giants. I had higher hopes for Mauigoa, a top-10 pick, but he has been good enough at right guard.

Sure, there was a sense of letdown as the NFL trade deadline came and went Tuesday and the Giants did not exactly jump into the deep end of the pool.

The lack of action was not for a lack of trying. The Giants engaged in "a lot of conversations," according to a source, but were wary about overpaying for players and eventually stood pat.

Which teams will look back and regret not doing more? I wonder how much the Ravens and Chargers mostly deciding to hold onto their veterans mattered here, given that Harbaugh has shown an affinity for adding players from those organizations (and front offices) he knows best since joining the Giants. It's also true that New York will get Cam Skattebo back from his hamstring injury sooner than later, but the Giants are relying heavily on a player who was a healthy scratch for them earlier in the year in Devin Singletary. They're one injury away from Greg Desrosiers Jr. stepping in as their primary running back.

Like the Rams, the Giants are probably going to look toward veteran free agency as a place to add depth. It wouldn't be a shock to see them bring in someone like Taven Bryan for a tryout at defensive tackle. Backs like Antonio Gibson and D'Ernest Johnson are still available in free agency. Given that they're in position to win the NFC East for the first time since 2011, though, I'm a little surprised that the Giants didn't make at least one move for a rotational player on offense or defense.

The Giants didn't make any big deals at the trade deadline, but they tried to make a very big one. The Giants called the Falcons to see whether running back Bijan Robinson was available in a trade, according to Jordan Raanan of ESPN.

That would have been a blockbuster, but it's unsurprising that the teams didn't make a deal. The Falcons made a big investment in Robinson this offseason, signing him to a three-year, $66.8 million contract extension, and from all accounts they view him as one of the most important pieces of their franchise.

#2900. Prediction markets tied to clinical trials and drug approvals draw lawmakers' ire

Prediction market event contracts on both clinical drug trials and Food and Drug Administration regulatory approvals are drawing attention from two Democratic senators, according to a letter obtained first by CNBC.

Sen. Angela Alsobrooks, D-Md., and Sen. Ron Wyden, D-Ore., expressed concern over the related event contracts in a Wednesday letter to Department of Health and Human Services Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. The senators asked what measures HHS has taken to mitigate any risks that may arise from the markets.

"Allowing individuals to bet on the success of these trials and drugs undermines the scientific process, creates perverse incentives for researchers, harms trial participants, and could lead to dangerous medical outcomes for untold numbers of Americans," the senators wrote.

Alsobrooks and Wyden repeated concerns raised by some healthcare professionals that the markets could alter patient thinking when considering whether to participate in a trial and expressed concern over potential insider trading risks from researchers and regulators who may have access to nonpublic information.

The senators requested HHS provide all internal communication regarding staff participation in prediction markets tied to matters governed by the department. They also requested all communication between the department and the prediction market platforms themselves, the Commodity Futures Trading Commission — which regulates event contracts — and any internal analyses that have been done regarding the potential impact of the markets.

"HHS, FDA, and [the National Institutes of Health] must uphold the integrity of the drug development process and maintain public trust in the public health by putting essential guardrails in place to prevent individuals profiting on the success and approval of drugs that the American people depend on," Alsobrooks and Wyden said in the letter.

Both leading prediction market platforms, Kalshi and Polymarket, offer the contracts, although in Polymarket's case the markets can only be found on its international exchange, which isn't regulated in the U.S.

When Kalshi launched its related markets in July, the company said it put in place the guardrails, such as only allowing markets related to late-stage trials, in order to limit the risk of trading. Kalshi also vowed to list contracts only after a trial had completed enrollment and to use employee verification technology for all traders participating in the markets.

"Drug development is one of the most important and most information-constrained industries on earth," Kalshi CEO Tarek Mansour said in July. "The data that determines which drugs advance and which don't is largely locked away from the people who need it most. Surfacing information is what Kalshi is for, and we are committed to doing it right."

Kalshi noted that investors also buy shares in companies in fields including biotechnology and pharmaceuticals that are awaiting regulatory approvals.

"The hypothetical concerns of the Senators apply equally to the equities market, which appears to be left out of their letter.," Kalshi spokesperson Jack Such told CNBC. "The only difference is that the size — and incentive for bad activity — is significantly higher in the equities market than it is on Kalshi."

Polymarket and the Department of Health and Human Services didn't immediately respond to CNBC's requests for comment.

Disclosure: CNBC and Kalshi have a commercial relationship that includes customer acquisition and a minority investment.

#2899. NFL Week 10 picks: Packers prevail over Vikings; Steelers beat Bengals

Los Angeles Times NFL writer Sam Farmer examines the matchups and makes his predictions for Week 10 of the NFL season.

All lines and over/under numbers are according to FanDuel Sportsbook.

Last week, Farmer posted an 15-0 record. Through the first nine weeks of the season, he is 89-47 (.654).

Using point spreads with the scores Farmer predicted, his record against the spread in Week 9 would have been 12-3 (.800). For the season, his record against the spread is 65-71 (.478).

All times are Pacific and TV reflects broadcasts in the Los Angeles area. The Bears, Broncos, Buccaneers and Eagles are off this week.

New York Giants running back Cam Skattebo carries the ball against the Houston Texans on Oct. 25. (Maria Lysaker / Associated Press)

The last time these teams met, they tied. The Commanders haven't won on the road this season, and the Giants are getting J.J. McCarthy and Cam Skattebo back. The Giants defense shines.

The Patriots defense started strong but has fallen apart, and last week it was embarrassed by Green Bay. Offense is beat up. Detroit isn't scoring a ton but has won four in a row.

Patrick Mahomes' elbow is in question. The Chiefs are coming off a get-right win over the Jets. The Falcons pulled off a stunner against San Francisco three weeks ago but that was their Super Bowl.

C.J. Stroud is coming off a five-touchdown game against the Chargers, and the Texans' defense had eight sacks. Deshaun Watson starts against his old team. The Browns have problems scoring in the red zone.

Green Bay Packers quarterback Jordan Love passes against the New England Patriots on Nov. 8. (Greg Cooper / Associated Press)

Can the Vikings solve all their QB problems by trading for Kirk Cousins? That's a big ask. Jordan Love is having a great season, and the Packers' defense is second in the league in takeaways with 19.

Jacksonville might be the best team in the AFC and it just beefed up its defense at the trade deadline. Cam Ward is coming off his best game for the Titans, but Jeffery Simmons being traded to Chicago is a major loss for the defense.

The Colts had a fire sale but at least they kept their quarterback and running back. That might be enough to hold off the Dolphins, who have played a bit better lately by keeping games fairly close.

Bryce Young looked good against Denver last week — much better than his two previous games — but the Panthers have still lost three in a row. The Saints are coming off a clutch performance in an overtime win over the Browns.

The Bills are coming off a short week but they have the NFL's highest-scoring offense. The Jets have lost four in a row and kick a lot of field goals. Jets can run the ball, though, and Breece Hall is back.

Rams quarterback Matthew Stafford passes against the Arizona Cardinals on Oct. 18. (Eric Thayer / Los Angeles Times)

The Rams aren't going to be swept by Arizona. They're in trouble at receiver, though, and need to find a way to limp through. Myles Garrett makes a scary defensive front terrifying. Tough draw for Carson Beck.

Fernando Mendoza makes his starting debut, and does so against a defense ranked No. 1 in yards allowed. Drew Lock starts again for the Seahawks, who are 2-0 with him at the helm.

The Cowboys can score but they can't stop anyone, least of all San Francisco, which might be the best team in the league. Despite a bunch of injuries (like a lot of teams) the 49ers keep finding ways to win.

Pittsburgh Steelers quarterback Aaron Rodgers scrambles against the Cleveland Browns on Nov. 1. (Matt Durisko / Associated Press)

First place in the AFC North is at stake, and these teams are evenly matched. Both have won four in a row, and Pittsburgh won the last meeting. The Steelers are 12-4 in Cincinnati since 2010.

The Ravens have lost four in a row and have a new quarterback in Jacoby Brissett. Hard to bet on the Chargers until they show a pulse, however. They've gotten into holes early and can't dig out.

#2898. Bouncing Wynn Shares might be primed for the 'wheel strategy'

In Barry Levinson's classic film Rain Man, Dustin Hoffman's character leans over to his brother in the middle of a blackjack hand with a simple piece of advice: bet two for good, one for bad.

In the case of Wynn Resorts shares, it might be time to bet two for good.

Steve Wynn hasn't exactly been on a winning streak. No longer running his namesake company, he finally sold his 50 Central Park South penthouse after years on the market. WYNN shares haven't really been on a winning streak either. The stock is down more than 27% year-to-date as I write this. It was down more than 46% from the 2025 highs within the past couple of weeks. But it has bounced a bit since then, and perhaps the tables are turning.

Fundamentally, the business is demonstrating exceptional strength, particularly in the United States. Wynn's Las Vegas EBITDA margin has actually surpassed that of its Macau resorts. Recognizing this, management has accelerated capital spending in Las Vegas, where the company's profit has doubled from pre-pandemic levels. Meanwhile, the Asian market is also showing signs of life. Strategic property upgrades in Macau are successfully drawing lucrative premium mass play, which is directly supporting the company's ambitious expansion plans for the Enclave at Wynn Palace.

From a valuation standpoint, WYNN is trading very close to its five-year historical averages across key metrics, including P/E, EV/EBITDA (currently around 9x to 10x), and Price-to-Book Value. Although this represents a slight premium compared to the broader gaming industry mean, Wynn's justifies a premium. (The Encore, the newer of the two adjacent Las Vegas properties, is one of my preferred hotels on "The Strip"). Technically, the stock has admittedly been in a downtrend, but this recent sharp bounce off the late October lows may indicate the run is over. Several technical indicators—specifically the MACD, Fear & Greed Index, Commodity Channel Index (CCI), and several key Moving Averages—are now aligned, suggesting it may be time to go long.

With the next earnings report not scheduled until February, the stock has a clear runway free of binary fundamental catalysts, yet implied volatility remains surprisingly high. This environment makes buying options expensive and short premium strategies more attractive.

Rather than betting on the roulette wheel, traders should look once again to "the wheel" strategy. Something I discussed earlier this week on United Rentals. The strategy? Step one, sell cash-covered puts below the current market price to collect that inflated premium. If WYNN stays above your strike price through expiration, you keep the cash and repeat the process. If the stock dips and you are assigned, you acquire shares at a discount. Once assigned, you move to step two: selling covered calls against your new stock position to generate continuous income until the shares are eventually called away, after which you revert back to step one and play again. A methodical, high-probability way to get long a world-class operator while getting paid to wait.

#2897. CFTC investigates if Kalshi improperly self-certified event contracts

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission is investigating whether prediction market platform Kalshi failed to properly inform the agency of proposed event contracts, Kalshi confirmed to CNBC.

Kalshi called it a minor issue in how the contracts were filed and said it is working with the CFTC to address the matter. The CFTC flagged weather-related contracts, Kalshi said. The leading prediction market platform didn't say when the agency began investigating Kalshi. CFTC Chairman Michael Selig told CNBC that the agency does not comment on investigations.

Politico first reported on the probe on Tuesday, noting charges could happen soon, based on the CFTC's private notification to Kalshi. The platform declined to comment on any possible charges.

Prediction market platforms are allowed to self-certify event contracts without the federal agency's prior approval, as long as they comply with the Commodity Exchange Act. The self-certification allows for faster introduction of new contracts.

"The notice is about a procedural error involving the submission of a handful of contracts, many of which weren't live," said Elisabeth Diana, head of communications at Kalshi. "We are already addressing the issue."

The move is the latest by the CFTC as it seeks to rein in prediction markets, which face growing scrutiny in Washington from regulators and members of Congress.

In August, the CFTC ordered Kalshi to take down sports-related "mention markets" — where speculators can trade on what individuals will say in a broadcast or speech — while it looked into the event contract type.

The agency is also conducting a broad probe into competitor Polymarket, which includes reviewing if the company violated a 2022 settlement with the CFTC to prohibit U.S. traders from accessing its international trading platform. Polymarket's domestic exchange operates separately from the global platform.

Prediction markets are also expected to face heightened scrutiny once the Democratic Congress is sworn into office in January. Rep. Ro Khanna, D-Calif., in a Tuesday social media post on X, called for a ban on sports prediction markets — which account for the overwhelming majority of volume on leading platforms.

— CNBC's Talia Kaplan contributed to this report

Disclosure: CNBC and Kalshi have a commercial relationship that includes customer acquisition and a minority investment.

#2896. 10-year Treasury yield jumps back above 5% as oil gains and more Fed rate hikes expected

U.S. Treasury yields jumped on Thursday, sending the 10-year note above 5% alongside a move higher in oil prices and expectations of further Federal Reserve hikes.

The yield on the 10-year U.S. Treasury note — the benchmark for mortgages, auto loans and credit card debt — rose 3.1 basis points to 5.02% after earlier hitting 5.0583%, the highest since Oct. 22.

The 2-year Treasury note yield, more sensitive to short-term Fed interest rate decisions, jumped 7 basis points to 4.797%. The longer-dated 30-year Treasury bond yield 1.4 basis points to 5.363%.

One basis point equals 0.01%, or 1/100th of 1%, and yields and prices move inversely to one another.

The rise in oil prices came after President Donald Trump said, "we don't need a deal with Iran," raising concern that the war in the Middle East will continue indefinitely. West Texas Intermediate futures gained 0.4% to $86.86 a barrel, while Brent crude was ahead about 1% to $93.60 per barrel.

Tuesday's hotter-than-expected consumer price index reading has investors anticipating more rate increases from the Fed. Goldman Sachs forecast another rate hike to its forecast on Wednesday, now expecting an additional quarter percentage point increase in March. Traders are pricing in 100% odds of an increase in December, with the chance of a half percentage point increase standing at about 13%, according to the CME Group's FedWatch tool.

Another inflation report is due Friday, when the October producer price index, which tracks wholesale prices, is expected to show a 0.4% rise in October, down from the 1.3% surge in September.

On Thursday, investors are looking for October's home sales report from the National Association of Realtors. Existing home sales rose 1.5% in September to 3.99 million, up from a two-year low in August, and are expected to have reached 4 million last month, according to Trading Economics estimates.

#2894. Ukrainian defense tech startup hits $6 billion valuation as investors bet on battle-tested drones

Ukrainian defense technology company UFORCE has raised $700 million in a funding round valuing it at $6 billion, as investor interest grows in autonomous weapons systems tested on the battlefield in Ukraine.

UFORCE, which develops unmanned aerial, maritime, and land systems as well as AI software used to coordinate them, raised $50 million at a valuation of around $1 billion in March.

The Series A round was co-led by Valor Equity Partners and the U.K.'s National Wealth Fund, with participation from investors across Silicon Valley, Europe and Asia, the company announced Thursday.

UFORCE CEO Oleg Rogynskyy told CNBC that the company's bookings have more than doubled over the past year, from a base of several hundred million dollars, but declined to disclose more exact figures.

He said the company's financial performance justified its latest valuation, adding that UFORCE had received offers from investors at even higher valuations.

"We were getting offers with much higher valuation, but we chose this one because of Valor and the rest of the investors," Rogynskyy said. “In terms of valuation to bookings, valuation to revenue, this is actually not an aggressive valuation.”

The company plans to use the new funding to scale manufacturing in Ukraine and in allied countries, including the U.S., Europe, Asia-Pacific and the Middle East.

Startups across NATO and allied countries raised $27.1 billion through September, nearly double the total for all of 2025, according to an October report from Dealroom and Resilience Media.

Rogynskyy said UFORCE had already manufactured ten times as many unmanned surface vessels as the competitors it typically measures itself against, and was looking to increase manufacturing capacity substantially. CNBC could not independently verify the comparison.

The expansion comes as militaries around the world look to learn from the war in Ukraine, where relatively inexpensive drones are challenging the role of traditional military equipment like crewed tanks or fighter jets.

UFORCE says its NEMESIS drones have completed more than 300,000 combat flights, while its MAGURA naval vessels have contributed to the destruction of more than a dozen Russian warships in the Black Sea.

Rogynskyy said the company was already working with several NATO countries, but declined to identify its customers or disclose how much of its production was being supplied to Ukraine versus overseas markets.

Asked whether growing international demand could divert supplies away from Ukraine, he said the company was committed to meeting Ukrainian military requirements first.

"We are very careful about that, and we make sure that we deliver on all Ukrainian needs first and foremost," he said.

Like many of its peers, UFORCE is seeking to challenge the economics of conventional warfare, arguing that large numbers of inexpensive autonomous systems can provide a significantly cheaper way of defense than more traditional weapons.

Rogynskyy said Ukrainian defense tech companies have an advantage over established Western manufacturers because their engineers receive continuous feedback from military personnel using their equipment on the battlefield.

"Speed of innovation, of course, is much faster with Ukrainian defense technology companies as compared to primes and Western defense techs," he said.

Looking further ahead, Rogynskyy predicted that autonomous systems would gradually replace much of the military equipment currently operated by humans, including aircraft and naval vessels.

He said he could not envision militaries continuing to purchase new crewed aircraft or frigates 10 years from now, although he acknowledged that conventional equipment such as F-35 fighter jets would remain important in the near term.
