#2870. Oil prices mixed after Trump says no Iran deal needed: 'We've got their Strait'

U.S. oil prices traded around the flatline Thursday, as comments from President Donald Trump stoked worries that an end to the U.S.-Iran war was not in sight.

Brent crude traded 0.5% higher at $93.29 per barrel at 9:09 a.m. West Texas Intermediate futures gained slipped 0.1% to trade at $86.05 per barrel.

"We don't need a deal with Iran. Those days have long since passed. We've got their money, Hundreds of Billions of Dollars, and we've got their Strait!" Trump said in a Truth Social post.

Thursday's gains follow a sharp pullback after Trump played down the prospect of further strikes on Iran after the midterm elections. WTI is down about 16% in November, according to data from FactSet.

On Sunday Trump said that fresh attacks were highly unlikely as talks to end the conflict progressed, despite both ⁠countries reporting that shipping had been hit in or near the Strait of Hormuz. On Monday, he said further military action did not appear necessary "at this point," easing concerns about prolonged disruptions to Middle East oil supplies.

John Kilduff, a partner at Again Capital, said investors may have been too quick to price in an end to the fighting.

"The market may have gotten ahead of itself on pricing in a cessation of hostilities," Kilduff told CNBC. He warned that renewed doubts about a deal would push the security premium in oil prices higher.

Trump's assertion that the U.S. controls the Strait of Hormuz could spur further Iranian attacks on shipping, increasing the risk of prolonged supply disruptions, according to Kilduff.

"The President may be thinking that given enough time, the economic blockade of Iran will force them to either negotiate with the USA or result in a regime change as the Iranian economy collapses. The Iranians might have different ideas and continue to attack Middle East Infrastructure that results in higher prices and forces the President to the negotiating table," said Andy Lipow, president of Lipow Oil Associates.

"In any event, it does not seem to me that either side's negotiating position has changed. Iran wants the blockade to end, sanctions to be lifted and a ceasefire including Lebanon to be declared. The USA wants Iran to get rid of its entire nuclear stockpile and allow free transit through the Strait of Hormuz," he said.

—CNBC's Pippa Stevens contributed to the report.

#2869. Nebius falls 10% as company expects increased 2026 capex spend, issues weak outlook

Nebius shares sank 10% on Thursday as one of the biggest publicly traded neoclouds reported third-quarter revenue that beat analysts' estimates, but issued disappointing guidance.

Here's how the company performed relative to LSEG consensus:

It was not immediately clear if the loss per share was comparable to the 69 cents expected by analysts.

Revenue jumped 571% in the quarter, according to a statement. The company reported a net loss of $37.5 million, a loss of 13 cents per share, compared with $119.6 million, a loss of 50 cents per share, a year earlier.

In a letter to shareholders, CEO Arkady Volozh said the company expects fiscal year 2026 revenue at the low end of the company's $3 billion to 3.4 billion range. FactSet expected full-year revenue of $3.35 billion.

Nebius also shared an initial outlook for 2027, expecting revenue of $12 billion to $13 billion, "reflecting a conservative approach to the pace of our build-out." FactSet expected 2027 revenue of $12.09 billion.

The company expects FY2026 capital expenditures of $27 billion, higher than its previous capex guidance of $20 billion to $25 billion and the FactSet expectation of $23.85 billion.

Nebius' expected FY2027 capex of $40 billion to $45 billion came in much higher than the FactSet expectation of $28.77 billion.

Nebius' results come after fellow neoclouds CoreWeave and IREN reported better-than-expected financials in their earnings announcements over the past week. That's provided a boost to the companies' stocks, which had suffered steep selloffs in recent weeks on concern about the sustainability of the artificial intelligence buildout.

The neocloud companies posted a robust rally on Wednesday, with Coreweave booming nearly 25%, Nebius climbing 15%, Applied Digital rising more than 7% and IREN adding nearly 6%.

Each of the stocks fell Thursday premarket after the Nebius Q3 report.

Last week, CoreWeave's bond yields spiked past the 16% mark. In its earnings report on Tuesday, CoreWeave told analysts it expects to keep parent-level debt financing under $5 billion through 2027.

The political backdrop has created further volatility as Nebius races rivals to open up new data centers. Last week U.S. voters supported dozens of ballot initiatives to restrict the development of such facilities, while Democrats won sweeping victories in both houses of Congress.

Then there's the Nvidia problem. Following stinging revelations about CEO Jensen Huang's affairs, including one with an alleged Chinese spy, the Wall Street Journal reported late Wednesday that Nvidia's board was planning to push out the company's co-founder.

Nvidia, which has developed the neocloud market by investing in the companies and backstopping some of their projects, held about $329 million in Nebius shares at the end of June, according to a filing.

During the quarter, Nebius said it raised $5.75 billion in debt financing.

Executives will discuss the results with analysts and issue guidance on a conference call starting at 8 a.m. ET.

#2868. Jensen and Lori Huang's Nvidia fortune is jointly controlled. How that makes things complicated now that he's resigned

A version of this article appeared in CNBC's Inside Wealth newsletter, a weekly guide to the high-net-worth investor and consumer. Sign up to receive future editions straight to your inbox.

Jensen Huang's $140 billion stake in Nvidia is largely held by trusts that are co-owned with his wife, Lori, creating added uncertainty for the shares and his financial future following his resignation from the company, according to attorneys and SEC filings.

Huang and his wife jointly own about 800 million Nvidia shares worth roughly $135 billion through a complex web of trusts and LLCs created for tax and estate planning purposes, according to SEC filings. Jensen Huang directly owns about 70 million shares valued at about $12 billion.

While owning company stock through a trust is common among tech founders and CEOs, it's less typical for a founder to have equal ownership with a spouse, tax experts say. The arrangement further complicates Huang's sudden exit from the company, but could prove to be a stabilizing force for the shares, since the trusts make it more difficult for either Huang or his wife to quickly sell.

"Each of Mr. Huang and his wife may be deemed to have shared beneficial ownership of, and to exercise shared voting and investment power over," according to an SEC filing.

According to the filings, Jensen and Lori Huang own about 530 million shares as co-trustees of the Jen-Hsun and Lori Huang Living Trust, created in 1995. They're also co-trustees of the Huang 2012 Irrevocable Trust, which owns 31.4 million shares, and the Huang Irrevocable Remainder Trust, which owns 109 million shares. About 135 million shares are held by LLCs that have the Living Trust as the sole member, according to the filings.

Lori Huang is the president of the Jen-Hsun and Lori Huang Foundation, which owns 69 million shares and reported $9 billion in net assets in 2024, according to IRS filings.

Each of the trust structures serves different tax or estate purposes, according to trust and estate attorneys. The living trust is likely a revocable marital trust, since it was created 10 years after the Huangs married and two years after Nvidia was founded in 1993.  Attorneys say living trusts help couples avoid the potentially expensive and public probate process  when one spouse dies. Many couples, for example, create living trusts for homes they purchase or other assets.

The main tax benefit of living trusts is that when one spouse dies, the surviving spouse gets a step-up in basis for the entire trust's assets, rather than just their half, according to attorneys.

While the rules of each living trust vary, attorneys say shares in the living trust can typically be sold or divided fairly easily in the case of a divorce.

"It's like a joint checking account," said Kathleen Martone, partner at Varnum LLP.

While the revocable trust would typically allow each of them to sell their shares, lawyers said it is common for executives or their trusts to have agreements with the company limiting the sale or transfer of shares to prevent large-scale liquidation.

"In the event they divide shares in their revocable trust, there's probably transfer restrictions. He may have to hold them for her benefit, and she would get the monetary benefit of the shares but not voting or management rights," said Leslie Evans, partner at Withers. "He could retain the stock while Lori receives other assets or an equalization payment."

The more complicated structures used by the Huangs, and the ones with the largest tax benefits, are the irrevocable trusts. The SEC filings state that "Mr Huang and his wife are co-trustees" of both trusts. With an irrevocable trust, the creator transfers ownership of an asset into the trust and the terms can't be changed without the consent of the beneficiaries or a court.

Attorneys say the 2012 irrevocable trust is likely an "intentionally defective grantor trust," a type of irrevocable trust where the creator gifts assets but is still treated by the IRS as the owner of the property for income tax purposes.

The structure allows the creator to lend money to the trust to buy the shares, as well as pay income taxes for the trust when it earns money. The result — an unlimited gift to the trust that escapes the estate tax.

What's more, the trust can avoid capital gains taxes on any appreciation of the shares in the trust.

"The trust principal is staying high, my own taxable personal assets are being used to pay the tax, and I'm able to funnel more and more money into the trust," said Azriel Baer, trust and estates partner at Farrell Fritz, P.C. in New York.

Their other irrevocable trust, the "Huang Irrevocable Remainder Trust," was likely created for the benefit of their children.

By definition, the trustees and terms of an irrevocable trust are difficult to change. Since Jensen Huang and Lori Huang are both co-trustees, neither can likely remove or sell the Nvidia shares held by the trust, experts say.

"An irrevocable trust is its own entity that has its own operational provisions, and so it has some estate and asset protection planning," Martone said. "Theoretically an irrevocable trust would be protected from a divorcing spouse as a creditor."

Of course, the protections all depend on the terms of the trust, attorneys say. Whether an irrevocable trust can be changed or "decanted" in legal parlance depends in part on state law. California, where the Huangs live, is strict when it comes to changing irrevocable trusts.

For Nvidia shareholders, the irrevocable trusts could provide stability for the shares, since it would be difficult for either Jensen or Lori Huang to remove the shares from the trust and sell them.

Attorneys say it's unlikely the Huangs would change a trust created for the benefit of their children.

"If (the trusts) are for benefit for kids, there's no reason to pull them back into their estate," said Rochelle Schultz, shareholder at Weinstock Manion.

The Inside Wealth newsletter by Robert Frank is your weekly guide to high-net-worth investors and the industries that serve them.

#2867. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2865. Trump says ‘we don’t need a deal with Iran’

The U.S. should no longer seek a deal with Iran, President Donald Trump said Thursday as he directed Vice President JD Vance to instead focus his attention on corruption and crime in Democratic cities and states.

"We don't need a deal with Iran. Those days have long since passed," Trump wrote in a Truth Social post.

"We've got their money, Hundreds of Billions of Dollars, and we've got their Strait!" Trump wrote, referring to the Strait of Hormuz, a key chokepoint in the ongoing U.S.-Iran war.

The announcement came just over a week after Republicans suffered huge losses in the midterm election. Vance and other Republicans have pinned those losses on the Iran war, which the U.S. and Israel started in late February.

In the weeks leading up to Election Day, Trump repeatedly suggested the U.S. could resume launching military strikes, which have largely subsided as a tense stalemate has taken hold in the region

The Wall Street Journal on Wednesday reported that Vance, who has led diplomatic efforts with Iran in prior months, was considering face-to-face talks with a top Iranian security official.

Stock futures declined following Trump's post.

The unpopular Iran war, which Trump and others in his administration said would be won in a matter of weeks, is now in its ninth month with no clear end in sight. The conflict region has driven up global energy prices, creating a ripple effect through the economy and exacerbating concerns about inflation and affordability that were paramount to voters in the midterms.

Corruption emerged as another top issue that broke in Democrats' favor in the election. Trump's post Thursday morning attempts to flip the script, blaming Democratic-run areas for allowing corruption and crime to proliferate.

"I have asked Vice President Vance to focus his complete and total attention on the Massive Corruption and Crime taking place in Democrat run Cities and States throughout the U.S.," Trump wrote.

"The Blue States, such as Minnesota, California, Illinois, and many more, are all poorly run, corrupt disasters. Billions of Dollars is being stolen each and every month," the president wrote. "Our Budget could be quickly BALANCED with the correction of this large scale criminal activity."

"CORRUPTION is in the Democrat Leadership's blood!" he added.

#2863. Nvidia stock falls nearly 10% as CEO Jensen Huang announces resignation

Follow CNBC's live coverage of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's resignation here.

Nvidia shares plunged nearly 10% on Thursday, sending the company's value below $4 trillion in market cap after Jensen Huang announced he would immediately step down as CEO and a member of the board at the chipmaker.

Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress will serve as interim CEO until a new leader is appointed, Nvidia said in a Thursday statement. Harvey C. Jones, former CEO of Synopsys, will become the first-ever chair of the board.

On Sunday, Huang admitted to having an affair. The CEO was romantically involved with Alice Huang, an executive at cloud company Megaspeed, according to The Financial Times, who U.S. intelligence officials believe is a spy for China.

The New York Times on Wednesday reported that Huang had another extramarital relationship with Kimberly Powell, vice president of health care at Nvidia. Powell reports to Huang, according to the Times.

Before Huang resigned, several Wall Street analysts told CNBC that this departure could lead the stock to fall 20%, given how critical he has been to Nvidia's success. Nvidia's stock is already down more than 20% over the last month.

Bernstein on Thursday slashed its price target on Nvidia's stock by half from $400 to $200.

Stacy Rasgon, a senior analyst at Bernstein, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" on Thursday that Huang's alleged affair with a direct report means it is "much tougher for the board to leave him in position."

Huang was central to Nvidia's rise, having co-founded the company in 1993 and was pivotal in transforming Nvidia from a gaming hardware player to the company at the center of the artificial intelligence boom.

Rasgon said it is unclear who could be the "visionary" to lead Nvidia into the future as new CEO.

"Jensen is Nvidia, right? He is the visionary. The reason they're in the position that they're in today is because he made decisions 10 years ago," Rasgon said. "Who is the right person now to make the decisions for the next 10 years? I don't intuitively know who that person would be."

Earlier, U.S. lawmakers had called for Huang's resignation, adding further pressure to the CEO and the company.

Richard Reyle, chief investment officer at Questar Capital Partners, said Nvidia's lack of a succession plan makes the stock "uninvestable."

"Nvidia doesn't have that person in place, and so it's going to be either a choice of keeping Jensen and being faced with months and months of being hauled in front of probably congressional boards and things to explain themselves and all kinds of interrogations," Reyle told CNBC's "Access Middle East."

"Or they're going to have to move on from him, and that's just going to make the stock uninvestable. That doesn't make the AI trade uninvestable, but it certainly makes Nvidia uninvestable."

— CNBC's Caleigh Keating contributed to this report.

Houston Rockets forward Kevin Durant Mandatory Credit: Jerome Miron-Imagn Images

#2860. Nets pound Pelicans for much-needed bounceback win

NEW ORLEANS — The Nets came to New Orleans desperate for a bounce-back win. And they made it look easy in the Big Easy.

What Brooklyn got was a bounce-back beating, pounding the pitiful Pelicans 145-111 before 15,043 at Smoothie King Center on Wednesday night.

In a matchup of slumping teams coming off humbling losses, the Nets were the ones who showed some steel.

They lost to the Mavericks by 37, while the Pelicans were stinging from the worst regular-season defeat in their history, a 51-point humiliation to Golden State.

Moritz Wagner (13) celebrates after making a basket during the Nets' 145-111 blowout win over the Pelicans on Nov. 11, 2026, in New Orleans. AP Photo/Tyler Kaufman

On Wednesday, a Brooklyn team that looked in disarray in Dallas put up their highest-scoring first half in years, led by as many as 41 points and welcomed back Egor Dëmin with a resounding rout.

It was a rare laugher for the Nets (3-8), a complete team victory. Even without point guards Nolan Traore and Mikel Brown Jr., the Nets handed out 30 assists to just 15 turnovers. They shot 57 percent and 20 of 41 from deep.

And Michael Porter Jr. and Julius Randle looked like the veteran leaders they're being paid to be. Not because they were good, but because they made others better.

The former had a team-high tying 22 points, while the latter was a plus-38, tying Stephon Marbury for the best in franchise history for a road game.

"I just told Ju, me and him [are] going to have to take it on ourselves to get us organized and be in the right place, be involved in the offense," Porter said. "So we were just trying to communicate more, make quicker decisions and get into things quicker."

"We've got a lot of capable guys. I don't know how many people are in double figures. It seemed like a lot, though. So we got capable dudes. And when we share the ball like that, everyone gets to have fun, feel like they're in a rhythm. We can be pretty good offensively."

It was eight in double figures, but six with at least 15 points — Dëmin, Porter Jr., Randle, Day'Ron Sharpe and backups Moritz Wagner and Noah Clowney — to tie a franchise record.

For a bench that has struggled, that kind of balance was key.

Julius Randle drives to the basket during the Nets' blowout win over the Pelicans. NBAE via Getty Images

Brooklyn came out of the blocks quickly, with Danny Wolf — who had another double-double — giving them a 12-5 lead.

It was 39-25 by the end of the opening period, their second-highest scoring first quarter of the last two years. It got better in the second half.

For much of the early part of this season, the Nets bench has struggled. On Wednesday night, they didn't just hold their own, but were additive. And that's even without their pair of 20-year-old point guards.

Wagner had seven points in the first quarter and 13 by the break.

He had a ferocious dunk on Jordan Poole for an and-one that put the Nets up 31-19, and followed with a nice drive and a flex afterward.

Brooklyn took an 83-66 cushion into intermission when Randle found Terance Mann for a last-second 3. It was their highest-scoring half since Dec. 21, 2022 (when they had 91 against Golden State).

Danny Wolf rises up for a layup during the Nets' blowout win over the Pelicans. NBAE via Getty Images

"We executed really well offensively in the first half, and when everybody touches the ball, everybody sees the ball go through the net, it's a lot more fun. You play with a lot of effort," Wagner said. "We've got to find a way to do it in games where the ball doesn't go through the rim as much, but of course that was an 80-point half and I think that was a lot of fun for everybody."

It became an outright laugher in the second.

A putback dunk by Noah Clowney (who had a bounce-back 16-point night) padded it to 104-77 with 3:36 left in the third.

And unlike Atlanta to start this trip — when they blew a 17-point lead and fell to the Hawks — this time the Nets actually built on it, padding it to 41.

"We've got to bring the same energy, same focus to every game," Sharpe said. "No matter the score, no matter what's happening, no matter who's out, we all got to approach the game the same way."

#2859. Lazio, gol di testa e difesa solida: resta il nodo degli avvii di ripresa

Sei reti arrivano di testa, come per la Roma; tre dei nove gol subiti sono invece maturati subito dopo l’intervallo.

La Lazio ha segnato 25 gol e ne ha subiti 9 nelle prime 11 giornate. Secondo quanto riportato da Il Messaggero, La squadra di Gattuso ha raggiunto 29 punti, superando il precedente primato biancoceleste di 28 nel 2017/18. Al rendimento offensivo e alla tenuta difensiva si accompagna però un dato da correggere: tre delle reti incassate sono arrivate nei primi cinque minuti della ripresa.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

I biancocelesti continuano a segnare nonostante le pressioni elevate e le assenze pesanti. Secondo Il Messaggero, per ritrovare una Lazio così incisiva nei primi 11 turni bisogna risalire al 1936/37, quando la squadra era guidata da Jozsef Viola.

Noslin e compagni trovano la rete attraverso azioni costruite, ripartenze e conclusioni dalla distanza. Sono arrivati anche sei gol di testa: tanti quanti quelli della Roma, un primato europeo secondo i dati riportati. Il totale delle reti è già a quota 25; la Lazio allenata da Sarri raggiunse quota 26 soltanto a inizio marzo.

Nei pareggi contro Milan e Napoli, i biancocelesti avrebbero potuto chiudere la partita già nel primo tempo. I dati sulle conclusioni descrivono però una squadra particolarmente efficace rispetto alle occasioni create: è decima in Serie A per tiri complessivi e nona per tiri nello specchio, mentre i 25 gol segnati sono otto in più dei 17 previsti dagli xG.

Pur senza un vero stoccatore, la Lazio beneficia degli inserimenti di Frattesi. Gattuso lo ha definito «uno dei migliori invasori d’Europa», sottolineando una qualità che contribuisce alla capacità realizzativa della squadra.

Un mese fa Gattuso ridimensionava il peso della gestione del pallone: «non mi interessa nulla, dobbiamo andare sempre in avanti». La crescita della sua Lazio passa, tuttavia, anche dal palleggio. A fine settembre la squadra era quindicesima nel campionato per possesso; dopo le percentuali registrate a ottobre è salita al sesto posto.

Quando affiora la stanchezza, i biancocelesti ricorrono alla manovra per mantenere il controllo del gioco. È successo anche dopo l’accelerazione del Napoli al rientro dall’intervallo: il tecnico ha riconosciuto la capacità dei suoi di «riequilibrare la partita» tenendo il pallone e allentando la pressione avversaria.

#2858. Fantasy football Start ‘Em, Sit ‘Em: 30 players to start or sit in Week 10

The 2026 NFL trade deadline passed ahead of Week 10, shaking things up for some fantasy managers as they look to make critical start- 'em, sit- 'em decisions.

The most notable trade involved the Minnesota Vikings acquiring quarterback Kirk Cousins to serve as an upgrade for their offense. Cousins' presence should raise the ceiling for players like Justin Jefferson, while his departure from the Las Vegas Raiders will raise questions about the viability of players like Tre Tucker and Ashton Jeanty.

Elsewhere, Jonathan Taylor remained with the Indianapolis Colts, dumping cold water on those who had been holding out in hopes of a trade that would turn Seth McGowan into the team's lead back. And fantasy managers will have to continue dealing with bye weeks that are knocking out the likes of Caleb Williams, D'Andre Swift, Bucky Irving and Saquon Barkley.

JUSTIN JEFFERSON: Kirk Cousins trade is good news for Vikings WR

Who can you start and sit in fantasy football for Week 10 of the 2026 NFL season? USA TODAY Sports outlines 30 players to trust or fade this week:

Jared Goff, Detroit Lions (vs. New England Patriots)

There may be temptation to sit Goff after he failed to throw a touchdown pass last week against the Dolphins, but he has a favorable Week 10 matchup against the Patriots. New England has allowed a league-high 2,256 passing yards to quarterbacks this season and just struggled to contain speed threats Christian Watson and Matthew Golden in Week 9. Goff should be able to hit some chunk plays with his talented receiving corps.

Matthew Stafford, Los Angeles Rams (at Arizona Cardinals)

Similar to Goff, Stafford has had a couple of quiet games in a row, posting just one passing touchdown in each of his last two outings. He should be better equipped for a big-time game against the Cardinals, who have allowed the second-most fantasy points per game (FPPG) to quarterbacks this season, and against whom Stafford has thrown multiple touchdowns in three consecutive games.

Bryce Young, Carolina Panthers (at New Orleans Saints)

After a couple of down games, Young enjoyed a strong showing against a Broncos defense that is adept at pressuring the quarterback, inspiring confidence he will continue to be a solid starter even in less-favorable matchups. The Saints have allowed the 11th-most FPPG to quarterbacks this season and Young should have plenty of opportunities to hit downfield shots off play action.

Audric Estime, New Orleans Saints (vs. Carolina Panthers)

Three teams have allowed at least 11 rushing touchdowns to running backs this season. The Panthers are one of them and they have surrendered at least one rushing touchdown in five consecutive games. Estime took over as the between-the-tackles leader for the Saints last week after Kendre Miller's release, so he can be trusted as a flex with touchdown upside – provided Travis Etienne Jr. remains out of the lineup.

Ollie Gordon II, Miami Dolphins (at Indianapolis Colts)

Gordon has cooled slightly after four consecutive games with a rushing touchdown, but he gets a nice matchup against the Colts in Week 10. Indianapolis has allowed the fourth-most FPPG to running backs this season and is one of four teams to allow 1,000 rushing yards.

Jadarian Price, Seattle Seahawks (at Las Vegas Raiders)

Zach Charbonnet figures to see more between-the-carries opportunities than Price, but the first-round rookie still saw nine carries and caught four passes for 54 yards against the Cardinals. His touch floor looks fairly solid, so he should be able to do damage against a Raiders defense that has allowed the third-most FPPG to running backs this season.

Corey Kiner, New England Patriots (vs. Detroit Lions)

Kiner looks poised to become New England's backfield leader with Rhamondre Stevenson (knee) and TreVeyon Henderson (illness) sidelined. The 24-year-old has averaged 4.9 yards per carry and his high-volume status should be enough to make him a quality flex play even against a Lions defense that has thrived against the run.

Jameson Williams, Detroit Lions (vs. New England Patriots)

The Patriots just surrendered a combined 17 receptions, 335 receiving yards and two touchdowns to the speedy duo of Watson and Golden in Week 9. Williams should be well-positioned to use his speed to get deep against a New England defense that has allowed the most FPPG to receivers this season.

KC Concepcion, Cleveland Browns (vs. Houston Texans)

The Texans figure to pressure Deshaun Watson often in Week 10, just as they did Justin Herbert in Week 9, but Houston is still susceptible to allowing big plays to receivers. They just surrendered catches of 58 and 50 yards to Quentin Johnston and KeAndre Lambert-Smith respectively last week and have allowed the second-most FPPG to receivers this season. Concepcion could similarly rip off some chunk plays thanks to his speed and elusiveness.

Deebo Samuel, San Francisco 49ers (at Dallas Cowboys)

Samuel returned to 49ers practice on Wednesday and may have a chance to suit up against the Cowboys. If he does, feel free to fire him up as a flex play against a Cowboys defense that has allowed the seventh-most FPPG to receivers this season.

Ryan Flournoy, Dallas Cowboys (vs. San Francisco 49ers)

Flournoy has averaged 3.8 receptions and 58.2 receiving yards over his last five games while establishing himself as the clear-cut No. 3 target in Dallas' offense. The Cowboys will likely have to throw a lot to keep their matchup with the 49ers close, so Flournoy could emerge as a high-upside flex, especially if George Pickens is limited in any fashion after missing last week with a hamstring injury.

Darren Waller, Carolina Panthers (vs. New Orleans Saints)

Waller hasn't been consistent this season, but he remains an imposing receiving threat, as he showed in Week 9, racking up eight catches for 90 yards and a touchdown against the Broncos. The 34-year-old gets another favorable matchup against a Saints defense that has allowed the sixth-most FPPG to TEs this year, so he should threaten to score again in Week 10.

Hunter Henry, New England Patriots (vs. Detroit Lions)

The Lions have been better against tight ends lately, but they have still given up a league-high 704 receiving yards and 11 receiving touchdowns to the position. That should give Henry a chance to emerge as a potential top-five play at tight end this week.

Baltimore Ravens (vs. Los Angeles Chargers)

There are precious few defenses that can't be started against the Chargers. Herbert has been sacked 31 times in eight games this season and has thrown at least one interception across those contests, making Jesse Minter's stop unit a high-floor play.

The Bills just put together an elite defensive performance against the Vikings, intercepting Carson Wentz three times and holding Minnesota to six points. Geno Smith and the Jets may be live to perform a bit better, but the Bills should still be able to do enough to emerge as a top-10 fantasy defense this week.

Jordan Love, Green Bay Packers (vs. Minnesota Vikings)

Love has been one of the league's most consistent quarterbacks this season and threw for 387 yards and two touchdowns against the Vikings in Week 1. Still, trusting him against a Minnesota defense that ranks No. 1 overall in pressure rate, per the NFL's Next Gen Stats, and has surrendered the fewest FPPG to quarterbacks seems like a risky proposition.

Fernando Mendoza, Las Vegas Raiders (vs. Seattle Seahawks)

It will be fun to see Mendoza make his first NFL start after the Raiders traded Kirk Cousins to the Vikings, but don't expect the Indiana product to have a great outing. The Seahawks are allowing just one passing touchdown per game to quarterbacks this season, so Mendoza has a low floor/ceiling combination in this matchup.

Drew Lock, Seattle Seahawks (at Las Vegas Raiders)

Lock has a solid matchup against the Raiders, but trusting him is risky. Seattle's backup quarterback is dealing with a nagging oblique injury, which could lead the Seahawks to lean more on their running game. The Seahawks may also be able to build a big lead if they can stymie Mendoza, so Lock may not have to throw too much in this matchup.

Tony Pollard, Tennessee Titans (vs. Jacksonville Jaguars)

Pollard posted 246 rushing yards and five rushing touchdowns across his two games leading into the Titans' bye, so it's hard to recommend sitting him. Still, the Jaguars are allowing just 89.3 rushing yards per game, fifth-best in the league, so the veteran's upside is limited in an unfavorable matchup.

Tyler Allgeier, Arizona Cardinals (vs. Los Angeles Rams)

Allgeier handled almost all of Arizona's running back work with Jeremiyah Love and Bam Knight out in Week 9, but he turned it into just 46 yards on 19 touches. Allgeier should continue to have a sizable workload in the Cardinals' backfield rotation, but he is too touchdown-dependent to trust against a Rams defense that has surrendered just three total touchdowns to the position.

Quinshon Judkins, Cleveland Browns (vs. Houston Texans)

The Texans are an odd defense in that they are allowing 103 rushing yards per game, which ranks 13th in the league, but they have only surrendered rushing touchdowns in three of their eight games. Granted, those touchdowns have come in waves, but it nonetheless bodes well for their chances of stopping Judkins, who has averaged just 3.3 yards per carry this season, rendering him a touchdown-dependent fantasy option.

Kaleb Johnson, Green Bay Packers (vs. Minnesota Vikings)

The Packers are without Josh Jacobs, which would ostensibly push Johnson into the team's lead running back role. That said, Green Bay has averaged a league-worst 68.2 rushing yards per game, so trusting Johnson against an elite Vikings defense doesn't seem like a great gamble.

Tank Dell, Houston Texans (at Cleveland Browns)

Dell posted four catches for 105 yards and a touchdown on a team-high nine targets and saw his snap share increase to 47.1% in the Texans' Week 9 win over the Chargers. Will he continue to get that type of snap split and target share? If so, he will become a weekly starter because of his deep playmaking capabilities. Still, we'll need more than a one-game sample size to trust him against a Browns defense that has been solid against receivers this season.

DK Metcalf, Pittsburgh Steelers (at Cincinnati Bengals)

The Steelers may have to throw a lot to beat the Bengals, but will that guarantee a big game for Metcalf? He has posted three or fewer catches in three of his last four outings and is catching a career-low 51.6% of his passes. That makes him hard to trust even in what could be a higher-scoring game.

Caleb Douglas, Miami Dolphins (at Indianapolis Colts)

The Colts have struggled this season, but they still have a good cornerback duo of Sauce Gardner and Charvarius Ward. That will make it hard for Dolphins backup quarterback Kyle McCord to consistently get the ball to Douglas.

Adonai Mitchell, New York Jets (vs. Buffalo Bills)

Game script could help Mitchell rack up targets against the Bills, but it's hard to recommend trusting him after he was shut out in two of his last three games. The presence of Omar Cooper Jr. and the pending return of Garrett Wilson will only continue to erode his ceiling.

Tucker Kraft, Green Bay Packers (vs. Minnesota Vikings)

Love has focused heavily on targeting Watson and Golden this season, leaving Kraft as the offense's tertiary option. He has still posted strong outings most weeks, but he will be hard-pressed to do that in Week 10 against a Vikings defense that has been far and away the best against tight ends this season.

T.J. Hockenson, Minnesota Vikings (at Green Bay Packers)

Hockenson's outlook should improve with Cousins joining the fold, but the impact might not be immediate. The Packers have allowed the fourth-fewest FPPG to tight ends this season, so this is probably a stay-away spot for the veteran.

New Orleans Saints (vs. Carolina Panthers)

Some may look at Bryce Young's recent numbers (14 sacks, four interceptions over his last three games) as a reason to back the Saints in this spot. However, Young just threw for 276 yards and three touchdowns against a strong Denver Broncos defense, so he could be in for a good outing against a porous Saints stop unit.

There may be temptation to start the Colts against Miami's McCord-ed offense, but it's hard to trust an Indianapolis team that has struggled to generate consistent pressure to deliver, even in a favorable matchup.

#2856. Player Grades: Lakers vs. Bucks

The start of this game had all the makings of a game in Milwaukee amidst a November road trip.

The Lakers entered the game with Quentin Grimes (right finger sprain) being the only player unavailable but left it with Luka Dončić rolling his ankle, Austin Reaves tweaking his left heel and Collin Sexton bloodied after an incidental headbutt with Ryan Rollins.

Despite the start and the injuries, Los Angeles took care of business and carries a six-game win streak into Phoenix to close out its first extended road trip of the season.

So, let's dive into the win. As always, grades are based on expectations for each player. A "B" grade represents the average performance for that player.

21 minutes, 0 points, 9 rebounds, 6 assists, 0-4 FG, +14

The box score didn't treat LaRavia the best this game, but his impact continues to defy counting stats. Fifty-fifty balls, defense, running in transition, all factors in the Lakers being a plus-14 while LaRavia was on the floor.

29 minutes, 10 points, 11 rebounds, 3 assists, 1 block, 1 steal, 4 fouls, 5-10 FG, +19

I thought Kessler started this game a bit too passive around the rim defensively and it set the tone for the rest of his game. Nonetheless, he was yet again a crucial conduit between the Lakers' defensive and offensive production.

23 minutes, 8 points, 5 rebounds, 3 steals, 2 turnovers, 3-6 FG, 2-4 3PT, +4

It took Thybulle a half to really make his impact felt, but he continues to be a steady producer on both sides of the ball. Steals, deflections and threes have defined his impact this season and it was the same tonight.

32 minutes, 32 points, 5 rebounds, 5 assists, 5 turnovers, 3 steals, 10-25 FG, 5-12 3PT, 7-10 FT, +21

Luka makes it nine straight 30-point games but tweaked his ankle with around four minutes left in the game and the Lakers up by 20. Dončić was good, again, but started this game with a questionable shot diet and committed some lousy turnovers.

Of course you take the good with the bad, but Dončić's start of the game held him back from this being another all-around great game.

25 minutes, 18 points, 5 assists, 3 rebounds, 3 steals, 1 turnover, 6-10 FG, 1-3 3PT, 5-5 FT, +23

Before leaving the game with his injury, Reaves was the ying to Dončić's yang, providing the Lakers with balance, a steady shot diet and conservative ball handling. If it weren't for the injury, Reaves was well on his way to yet another efficient 20-plus-point outing.

26 minutes, 23 points, 3 rebounds, 2 assists, 9-15 FG, 5-8 3PT, +21

Mamu is stringing together some of the best games of his Lakers tenure. His connectivity was on full display tonight, draining threes and seemingly switching onto everyone on defense. His ability to relieve stress both in transition and in the half-court on offense remains invaluable.

Should Reaves or Dončić miss time, having someone like Mamu is a luxury.

20 minutes, 9 points, 5 assists, 2 steals, 4-5 FG, 1-1 3PT, +11

Speaking of continued production, Williams remained a consistent bench player tonight. His defensive intensity, especially on Tyler Herro leading to a scuffle, is an important aspect in keeping an otherwise below-average group above water. It also helps when he makes his threes.

13 minutes, 10 points, 3 assists, 1 rebound, 1 turnover, 1 steal, 4-8 FG, 0-3 3PT, 2-2 FT, +1

It's been an up-and-down season for Sexton thus far, but he continues to show improved poise when handling the ball in the non-Luka minutes. However, it still feels like he takes up a little too much of the usage when sharing the floor with Reaves.

13 minutes, 0 points, 2 rebounds, 2 turnovers, 0-3 3PT, -13

This road trip was good for the rookie, who got some real minutes in a couple of games. Tonight was evidence that Carr is still getting his footing and adjusting to the pace of the game. Aside from diving on the floor for a ball he lost, I feel we're still yet to really see how much of an impact he can have on the floor.

Vando played just nine minutes, but each of them was impactful. He brought instant energy in his first shift and continues to show improved aggression around the rim. He's had four points.

James, Hardy and Looney each played four minutes of garbage time after Dončić rolled his ankle and, yet again, almost blew a lead. Each of them was a minus-13 in their shift. Hardy has the greenest light in the NBA, James remains passive and susceptible to pressure and Looney took the floor for the first time in weeks (give him some grace).

Redick admitted that tonight could have easily been a trap game given how the team started. Many will also dock him for keeping Dončić in the game with four minutes left while up 20 when he eventually tweaked his ankle, but his explanation that the star wouldn't have been out there if Sexton didn't go down was reasonable.

The Lakers are on a seven-game win streak and Redick is pushing all the right buttons.

#2855. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2854. Best available free agent edge rushers for Bengals to target after failed Arnold Ebiketie trade

The Cincinnati Bengals rarely make a trade at the deadline, which is why it was somewhat surprising that a trade was reported on Tuesday when they (briefly) acquired Arnold Ebiketie from the Eagles.

However, the trade seems to have dissolved behind the scenes after the team realized the deal would also cost them a third-round compensatory pick they are hoping to receive from Trey Hendrickson signing with the Ravens. Then, for the rest of the deadline, no more moves were made, although it is unknown if any more trades were attempted or if they elected to stand pat once the Ebiketie deal fell through.

The attempted trade for Ebiketie, though, is a clear indicator that the Bengals are not happy with the depth of their defensive end room. Cashius Howell is on IR for an unknown amount of time, and Shemar Stewart is getting few snaps and was even a healthy inactive for a game.

With all of that, the failed trade, and no more trade possibilities, the Bengals could target the position through one of the free agents still available on the market, so let's take a look at the top available defensive ends.

The 31-year-old Bosa was one of the top edge rushers in the NFL for most of his career; he last played in Buffalo in 2025 on a one-year, $12 million deal. In the offseason, he was in the discussion for many fanbases, but no rumored meetings or potential signings popped up, and in the summer it was hinted he may have even quietly retired, with Adam Schefter saying he may have played his final snap.

However, in the last month there has been more buzz about him possibly coming back and joining a team for a run to the Super Bowl, mainly his previous team, the Bills, or joining his brother Nick Bosa in San Francisco.

It is not impossible to imagine, though, a world where he wants to join his close friend Joe Burrow in Cincinnati for a title run as well, and the Bengals should not hesitate to call and see if it is possible.

The 34-year-old Floyd is another older pass rusher who was productive relatively recently, now getting a lot of buzz as a potential addition for teams looking to make a Super Bowl push. On a one-year, $10 million deal in 2025, Floyd had a decent year and can still get involved as a pass rusher.

The Bengals should be a desirable destination for any free agent edge rushers with their talented offense and impactful interior, giving edge rushers a lot of one-on-one matchups in obvious passing situations. As a rotational pass rusher, Floyd would fit exactly what the Bengals need.

The 32-year-old Reddick was one of the best pass rushers in the league for several years and was a double-digit sack player with Philadelphia. His production in recent years has really taken a nosedive, but the Bengals could sign him with the hope that he has fresh legs, not playing most of the year, and could be effectively deployed in a limited capacity to keep him fresh.

If Reddick could find something even close to his 2023 form, he could be extremely impactful.

The youngest player on the list by a long shot, 28-year-old Yetur Gross-Matos signed a two-year deal with the 49ers in free agency in 2024. During that contract, though, he missed roughly half of his games with the team and wasn't a consistent performer in the games he did play in.

Gross-Matos did have flashes, and with him now either healthy or very close to it after a knee injury last year, he could be one of the more intriguing options, since he is still young and could have several years of impactful football ahead of him.

The Bengals have quite a few options available if they want some fresh legs and some help in the pass-rushing department. If they were willing to spend a sixth-round pick, they should be more than willing to spend a few million dollars if it is the difference-maker in getting the Super Bowl win.

What free agent defensive end would you target?

#2853. Rapid Recap: Lakers 123, Bucks 112

The Milwaukee Bucks put up an early fight against the Los Angeles Lakers but ultimately fell short, losing 123-112. Tyler Herro led all Bucks scorers with 27 points and seven assists, while Jaime Jaquez Jr.—coming off the bench for the first time this year—dropped 25 points. Luka Doncic led the Lakers with 30 points, while Sandro Mamukelashvili led the Lakers' second unit with 23 off the bench.

Earlier in the day, it was announced that Myles Turner would rest, Kel'el Ware would start, Nate Ament would start at SF, and Tyler Herro would be available. That lineup got off to a hot start, with Herro hitting a mid-range floater before Brayden Burries showed incredible body control, scoring over Walker Kessler with a tricky little finger roll. Of course, Luka Doncic needed just four minutes to score nine points—and he hardly broke a sweat—as the Lakers pulled out a 14-7 lead. Jenkins began rotating through the bench early, and tried several lineups and looks that kept LA on their toes. One of these rotations was Jaime Jaquez Jr., who seemed to have a respawn point under the hoop and, almost at Luka speed, scored eight points to keep the game within one possession for the rest of the first. Bucks down 30-28 after one.

Milwaukee stayed aggressive on defense to open the second, which helped keep the Lakers within striking distance. After LA built their lead back to eight, came the highlight of the night: Nate Ament posterizing Kessler on the fast break to snap the cold streak. Whew. Jaquez continued his effective play, matching Herro through the middle of the second with 13 points to lead the team in scoring. However, Milwaukee then hit their usual cold stretch, allowing the Lakers to piece together a 7-0 run that pushed their lead back to nine before the half. Accordingly, Herro had to check back in to quell the run, matching Luka's 19-point half with 18 of his own. The Lakers would not slow down, though, and the Bucks went into the locker room down 50-61. Despite the hot-and-cold shooting, there was a lot to like from the first half, including the Bucks almost matching the Lakers' shot total (44 to 46) and matching the assist total with 14.

Both teams got off to a slow start in the third because of disruptive wing defence. The Bucks, however, were less effective at closing out on defensive possessions, which allowed the Lakers to get multiple shots per possession and turn the tide. Their lead grew to 17 by the 5:20 mark, and as Milwaukee announcer Marques Johnson alluded to, the Bucks were trying to hold on tight to a rope that kept gradually being pulled away. And yeah, that is what it felt like: Milwaukee didn't exactly give the game away, but they didn't help themselves either, with too many defensive rebounds slipping away. Other than Herro and Jaquez Jr., no other Bucks player reached double figures by the end of the third, and the Bucks were now well behind at 73-94.

Former Buck Sandro Mamukelashvili opened the fourth quarter with a driving layup to bring his point total to 23, proving to be a real difference-maker. Milwaukee just couldn't find its groove, shooting 2/11 through the first six minutes, with the deficit still hovering around 20. By then, the Bucks had scored just six second-chance points for the game (!) to the Lakers' 31, illustrating their lack of front-court presence. During garbage time, however, Milwaukee stitched together a 13-0 run, which at least showed some continued hustle from a young team, but it ultimately masked another blowout loss.

Nine: The number of first-quarter assists for the Bucks. For the rest of the game, the Bucks notched just 16.

The Bucks need a primary playmaker, and while the recent returns haven't been stellar, Milwaukee should consider starting Jakucionis to see if he can set the table and help others find their shots. There really isn't a single player who would not benefit from the Bucks having a primary playmaker; someone who can help control the tempo and distribute the ball. This year will be all about development, and the Bucks need to see if Jakucionis can fill a reliable true point guard role.

#2852. ‘Never Knew This Rule Existed,’ ‘Kind of Genius’ — CFB Fans React As Miami (OH)’s ‘3D Chess’ Move Out of Bill Belichick’s Playbook Draws Penalty

Tuesday night MAC football apparently requires keeping an eye on the video board, too.

Miami (OH) put multiple images of goalposts on the screen behind the actual uprights while Ohio lined up for a field goal. The kick missed. Then came the flag, a referee's explanation, and a whole new appreciation for how creative home-field advantage can get.

The unusual sequence happened late in the first half of Miami's Nov. 10 matchup with Ohio. Bobcats kicker Brack Peacock was attempting a 34-yard field goal with his team ahead 17-7 when the extra uprights appeared behind his target.

Officials assessed Miami an unsportsmanlike conduct penalty, giving Ohio another opportunity before halftime.The clip quickly prompted a mixture of admiration, confusion, and questions about a rule plenty of viewers apparently had never encountered.

"Never knew the rule existed but I get it,"one fan wrote.

PREDICT THE CFB SEASON: PFN's Free CFB Playoff Predictor

"This is actually kind of genius if it works. You miss 100% of the shots you don't take. I salute you, Miami – Ohio,"Babu wrote.

There was certainly some imagination involved. Kickers already have the snap, hold, rush, and conditions to account for. A screen full of additional targets adds quite a visual to that routine, even if the missed kick alone cannot establish that the display caused it.

"How bizarre that someone even thought to write this into a rule book!"another fan added.

The relevant provision is NCAA Rule 9-2-1-b-5, which covers unsportsmanlike conduct. Its restrictions extend to audio, video, and lighting operators creating distractions that obstruct play or prevent a team from hearing its signals.

The people running the stadium presentation are part of that equation.

For some viewers, the scene brought back memories of New England's efforts to make life uncomfortable for visiting kickers.

"This is a trick that Belichick in the NE Patriots used to do. They would actually show reverse angle of the field goal attempt as the kicker was kicking,"one fan wrote.

The comparison follows renewed attention to thePatriots' video-board illusion, with footage showing a different view of the uprights behind the real ones. Miami's display featured multiple sets of posts, giving the college version its own unmistakable look.

Another fan pointed to an older college example.

"Huh? The Bills? And it's been a NCAA rule since 2020 because of Oklahoma State (v UT),"the fan wrote.

Oklahoma State did display yellow lines behind Texas kicker Cameron Dicker during a 2020 game, and the Big 12 subsequently said the tactic was not permissible.

The NCAA added video-board and lighting operators to the relevant unsportsmanlike-conduct language for the 2021 season, but NCAA rules editor Steve Shaw described the change as proactive rather than as a response to the Oklahoma State incident.

DIVE IN: PFN's Free CFB HQ — Team and Player Stats, Info, and More

Back in Oxford, Ohio received first-and-goal at the 8-yard line after the penalty. Miami's defense broke up the ensuing pass, keeping the Bobcats from adding points before halftime.

The RedHawks went on to win 34-17, so the unusual flag never became a costly turning point. The defense handled the extra opportunity, and the offense supplied plenty of points after the break.

By the end of the night, Miami had a rivalry win and a video-board operator whose work had attracted considerably more attention than the usual stadium graphics.

There isn't a ton that hasn't already been said about Cooper Flagg. The guy is an unbelievable player already, and there are still levels to go. The outside shot opens up so much more inside the arc for him to operate and get downhill. It'll be worth watching Flagg in this one, as the Wolves have the length on the perimeter and Gobert in the middle to potentially fluster Flagg.

#2849. Commanders look to stay alive against Giants in 'TNF' game Jayden Daniels called 'must win'

Fueled by defeat, Washington Commanders quarterback Jayden Daniels was not shy about labeling his team's next game.

"Must win," Daniels told reporters after the Commanders' 20-13 loss to the Los Angeles Rams on Sunday.

That date with destiny has arrived 96 hours later, with his team facing the New York Giants on "Thursday Night Football" Nov. 12 (8:15 p.m. ET, Amazon Prime Video). But head coach Dan Quinn, facing job-security questions with his team sitting at 2-5-1 and heading into a mini-bye, does not see the game in the same light.

"For me … I try to avoid the declarations of that. I've been around the league long enough to know that lots of season to play and lots of football to go," Quinn said.

Not afraid to speak for everybody in the organization in this instance, Quinn offered a different way to categorize it.

"We need this and we're battling for it," Quinn said. "That's really where the focus is, but we don't get into the big declaration stuff."

During the Week 5 meeting between the two teams, it was the lack of an outcome – the two teams played the lone tie in the NFL this season – that seemed to doom the Commanders' season. Daniels and the offense mismanaged the clock with no timeouts remaining in the final minute of overtime, and the clock expired before the team could kick the game-winning field goal.

"We know that the Giants," Daniels said this week. "They have a lot of good athletic players out there that they like to get into the mix and blitz, you know, try to create mismatch and matchups and stuff like that. So, we just gotta prepare for that and then really adjust from there on out."

Now another prime-time matchup against a division rival presents an additional high-profile assignment in a season defined by bad optics. The following week, on "Monday Night Football" against the San Francisco 49ers, the Commanders once again appeared confused – this time about what down it was – when Daniels threw the ball away on fourth down. Quinn's reaction did not assuage the critics of his game management. Neither did the call on the final play of the Rams' loss when the ball did not reach the end zone despite trailing by a touchdown.

A win against the Giants would move Washington two games back in the loss column and keep the Commanders alive in the NFC East race, with the Giants using backup quarterback J.J. McCarthy, who enters the matchup nursing a hip pointer that limited him last Sunday against the Philadelphia Eagles.

Part of the Commanders' slow start is tied to Quinn's two first-year coordinators, David Blough (offense) and Daronte Jones (defense). Quinn shot down speculation that he could make staff changes with another loss. Then there is the question of Quinn's own job security, especially if the Commanders find themselves.

"I'd say we're all frustrated," Quinn said.

What encouraged Quinn during practice this week was the offense having zero incompletions prior to the game and executing during the situational periods – red zone and third down. Washington ranks 23rd in red-zone touchdown drives and last in third-down conversion rate. Luckily for the Commanders, the Giants are last in red-zone defense, allowing a touchdown on nearly four out of every five drives that reach their 20-yard line (78.95%).

"He's still growing as a coordinator, first-time coordinator, still going through his growing pains and stuff like that," Daniels said of Blough. "So, I just try to do the best I can, just go out there, execute, doesn't matter what the play call is, it doesn't matter anything. If we got all 11 people going in the same direction, we have a chance."

#2846. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2845. Sean McVay laughs as he says he's tempted to ask assistant coach Robert Woods to suit up

He didn't sound completely serious, but Rams coach Sean McVay said on Wednesday that he talked to assistant wide receivers coach Robert Woods about whether he could suit up and play while receivers Puka Nacua, Davante Adams and Jordan Whittington are all out with injuries.

McVay was asked during his press conference whether he has talked to the 34-year-old Woods, who was a receiver for the Rams for five years, about potentially playing again.

"I have, yes," McVay answered with a laugh.

McVay acknowledged that there are Collective Bargaining Agreement rules against a coach becoming a player, but he said Woods is still in great shape and it would be fun to give it a try.

"I've looked at him, and the way he looks out there, I've definitely been tempted," McVay said. "I think that'd be a violation of the CBA. I don't know what he'd have to do, but he looks damn good and still is smooth out there. Very tempting."

Woods was cut by the Steelers before the 2025 season and hasn't played since, and he announced his retirement and signed a one-day contract with the Rams in February of 2026. Realistically, even with his intimate knowledge of the Rams' offense, he probably wouldn't be able to contribute as much on the field as whatever receiver the Rams could add off waivers or another team's practice squad.

If Woods and the Rams actually wanted him to become an active player, it wouldn't be as simple as signing him to the active roster, as the league has rules preventing that. Players on the reserve-retired list have to pass through waivers before they can join a team. And the NFL generally doesn't allow coaches to become players: In 2020, when the Broncos lost all their quarterbacks to covid, they asked the league office if they could activate one of their assistant coaches and were told no. In 2009, Eagles coach Andy Reid wanted to play coaching intern Matt Nagy at quarterback in a preseason game and was also told by the league that he couldn't.

So the idea of Woods playing receiver for the Rams is probably not going to get any kind of serious consideration. Even if, with all the Rams' injuries, it's tempting.

#2844. NFL Week 10: How to watch every football game this week during the 2026 season

If you buy something through a link in this article, we may earn commission.

Week 10 of the 2026-27 NFL season is here, featuring matchups between the Washington Commanders and New York Giants, Los Angeles Chargers vs. Baltimore Ravens, and the full slate of Sunday games.

Are you ready to watch some football? This season, NFL games will air across 10+ channels and streaming platforms. If you're worried about the challenge of following your favorite team through the upcoming season, from the NFL season opener to the 2027 Super Bowl, Yahoo Sports has you covered. Here's your playbook for how to watch the 2026-27 NFL season.

TV channels: ESPN, NBC, CBS, Fox, ABC and NFL Network

Streaming: Prime Video, Peacock, ESPN+, Paramount+, NFL+, Netflix

Washington Commanders vs. New York Giants: 8:15 p.m. ET (Prime Video)

New England Patriots vs. Detroit Lions (International Game): 9:30 a.m. ET (FOX)

Kansas City Chiefs vs. Atlanta Falcons: 1 p.m. ET (CBS)

Jacksonville Jaguars vs. Tennessee Titans: 1 p.m. ET (FOX)

Los Angeles Rams vs. Arizona Cardinals: 4:05 p.m. ET (CBS)

Seattle Seahawks vs. Las Vegas Raiders: 4:05 p.m. ET (CBS)

San Francisco 49ers vs. Dallas Cowboys: 4:25 p.m. ET (FOX)

Pittsburgh Steelers vs. Cincinnati Bengals: 8:20 p.m. ET (NBC/Peacock)

Los Angeles Chargers vs. Baltimore Ravens: 8:15 p.m. ET (ESPN/ABC)

Many NFL games are broadcast on local channels, so if you're looking to catch an in-market game, it may be as simple as turning on your TV (or setting up a digital TV antenna) or finding a live TV streaming service that carries the correct RSN (Regional Sports Network). A $7 monthly subscription to NFL+ will let you watch every local and primetime game live on your phone — but only a select few regular-season games on your TV. You could also spring for the uber-expensive NFL Sunday Ticket package to get every out-of-market Sunday game of the season.

When it comes to nationally broadcast games, the NFL typically airs on ESPN, NBC, CBS, Fox, ABC and NFL Network. Thursday Night Football games stream exclusively on Amazon Prime Video; select football games stream exclusively on Peacock; games on CBS stream live on Paramount+ Premium; and select games stream on ESPN's app this season. For those keeping count, that's six channels and four streaming platforms to keep up with. Plus, Netflix is once again hosting at least two Christmas Day matches, a playoff game and the first-ever Thanksgiving Eve battle, so don't forget to add that subscription into the mix.

Confused? You're not alone. Here's a breakdown of the platforms we recommend checking out so that tuning into your favorite team's games will be as easy as simply turning on the TV.

To recap, here are all the ways you can watch NFL games in 2026.

YouTube TV gets you nearly every channel you need to watch the NFL, including Fox, NBC, ABC, CBS, ESPN and NFL Network. Plus, YouTube TV is the home of NFL Sunday Ticket. The Sunday Ticket package, while pricey on its own, gets you ALL the Sunday out-of-market games (160+ games per season).

Current pricing for new users for the 2026 Sunday Ticket is $27/month for four months. Factor in the cost of YouTube TV's Sports and News plan at $49.99/month for your first five months, then $71.99/month thereafter.

That's $76.99/month for the next four months of YouTube TV + NFL Sunday Ticket. You can also opt, with or without a YouTube TV subscription, to pay for the rest of the season of Sunday Ticket upfront on its own for $108.

Fubo gives you access to ESPN, NFL Network, Fox, ABC, CBS, NBC, and 20+ more live channels. Starting at $63.99/month for your first month, the live TV streaming service is definitely pricey (though certainly not the most expensive option on this list), but it offers most channels you'll need to watch both the NFL preseason and regular season.

DirecTV's Choice tier (currently $77.98/month after fees for your first month, $97.98/month for the next two months and $107.98/month after) gets you access to nearly every channel you need to watch NFL games throughout the year: NFL Network, ESPN, NBC, CBS, Fox, SEC Network, and an abundance of local RSNs. DirecTV Choice (and most other DirecTV plans) also come with free ESPN Unlimited, which gets you access to every ESPN channel, an amazing value and a must for watching tons of college and pro sports all year round.

DirecTV offers a free trial on any tier, so you can try it all out at low risk. If you're interested in trying a live TV streaming service but aren't ready to commit, we recommend starting with DirecTV.

Amazon Prime Video is home to the NFL's Thursday Night Football. On top of Amazon Prime Video, an Amazon Prime subscription includes free shipping, exclusive deals, access to the Prime Day sales events, Amazon Music, a year of free GrubHub+ and more.

A standard Amazon Prime subscription is $15 monthly or $139 annually, but discounts are available for students and those on qualified government assistance. You can try Amazon Prime free for 30 days.

A basic $6.99 per month (or $49.99 annually) NFL+ subscription will get you live local and primetime regular and preseason NFL games exclusively on your phone or tablet, plus live audio for every game of the season across supported devices and access to NFL Network.

NFL+ Premium, while a little pricier at $14.99 per month (or $99.99 annually), also includes access to NFL RedZone, plus full and condensed replays of every game across supported devices.

The third-year forward is averaging 17.9 points, 4.9 assists and 4.4 rebounds through 11 games. His ability to handle the ball alongside Young, create for teammates and score from all three levels has given Washington another reliable offensive option.

AJ Dybantsa is averaging 16.7 points and 6.1 rebounds in seven appearances. The No. 1 overall pick has continued to show his scoring ability as he adjusts to the NBA, giving Washington another young player capable of making an impact offensively.
