#2840. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#2836. Marcus Smart throws punch at Warriors player, ejected from Rockets game

Houston Rockets guard Marcus Smart was ejected after he threw a punch and just barely grazed the face of Golden State Warriors forward Charles Bassey.

The incident came with 21 seconds left to play in the third quarter of a game Wednesday, Nov. 11, during a stoppage.

Just before the punch was thrown, Smart and Bassey got tangled up under the rim when trying to box out during a Warriors possession. The two continued to jaw at each other up the floor. Then, when play halted, Smart squared up and threw a right-handed hook that nearly landed on Bassey's cheek. Bassey had moved his head backward to avoid contact. Officials later said that Smart made contact to Bassey's nose.

One official immediately grabbed Bassey and separated him, while players from both teams swarmed around Smart to deescalate. Several Warriors players on the floor also took exception to the punch and were shouting at Smart, who then walked off the floor and headed straight into the locker room, surely aware that he would be tossed for the gesture.

Officials then reviewed the sequence and determined that Smart would draw a flagrant foul 2, which draws an automatic ejection.

Smart scored 6 points on 2-of-2 shooting and added 2 rebounds and 1 assist prior to his removal. Bassey was not disciplined whatsoever for his role in the matter.

Rockets coach Ime Udoka relayed Smart's view of the altercation after the game.

"Marcus, I just talked to him, he said they got wrapped up and his elbow that he had hurt got bent up. And so he said something to him and Bassey said 'What you going to do?' and he did it," Udoka said.

Bassey shared his view of the altercation postgame as well. He called Smart's play throughout the game "dirty" and said the altercation was Smart showing "fake toughness."

#2835. McIlroy makes strong start in bid to equal Montgomerie's record

DP World Tour Championship first-round leaderboard

-5 J Luiten (Ned); -4 JS Olesen (Den); -3 V Perez (Fra), G De Leo (Ita), F Schott (Ger), F Laporta (Ita), R McIlroy (NI)

Selected others: -2 T Hatton (Eng); -1 D Hillier (NZ), J Rahm (Spa); E E Chacarra (Spa), M Wallace (Eng); +2 E Ferguson (Sco), R Fox (NZ), C Jarvis (SA), J Schaper (SA); +3 P Reed (US); +4 T Fleetwood (Eng), A Rai (Eng)

Rory McIlroy made a strong start to his bid for a record-equalling eighth Race to Dubai title with a three-under-par opening round at the DP World Tour Championship.

The world number two, whose round included five birdies and two bogeys in windy conditions, is two shots behind early leader Joost Luiten.

Spain's Eugenio Chacarra, who leads McIlroy by 533.93 points in the Race to Dubai standings - with 2,000 available to this week's winner - opened with a level-par 72, while third-placed American Patrick Reed struggled during a 75.

"I think it was a pretty fair score," said six-time major winner McIlroy.

"I could probably stand here and argue that I could have been better, but I could probably stand here and argue that I could have been worse as well.

"I hit the ball well for the most part. It's tricky, the wind makes it tricky. The course set-up is a little more difficult than in previous years.

"The greens are not like Yas Links (in Abu Dhabi) last week. They're not absolutely perfect, so you've got to have your patience a little bit on them too. Overall, happy with the score."

Nine players - Chacarra, McIlroy, Reed, Casey Jarvis, Aaron Rai, Jon Rahm, Ryan Fox, Jayden Schaper and Daniel Hillier - can still clinch the Race to Dubai, but only Chacarra and McIlroy can guarantee the season-long accolade with victory at Jumeirah Golf Estates this week.

Northern Ireland's McIlroy, who is aiming to match Scotland's Colin Montgomerie's record of eight season-long crowns, birdied two of his opening four holes before giving a shot back at the fifth.

He birdied the seventh and 11th, and while he took six at the par-five 14th, he finished with a two-putt birdie four to end joint third.

"You can't win it on the first day, but you can certainly lose it," added McIlroy.

"I played nicely, so there was never any threat of me shooting a bad one.

"But it was possible today to go out there and shoot a couple over and feel like you were sort of out of the tournament.

"To get off to a solid start was very important."

Chacarra, who has won three times already this season, finished his front nine with three straight birdies to turn in two-under 34 but dropped shots at 13 and 15 to lie five back of Luiten.

Rahm, who catapulted himself into contention last week in Abu Dhabi, by winning his first tournament since leaving LIV Golf, mixed four birdies and three bogeys in an opening 71 to sit alongside New Zealand's Hillier.

Jarvis (+2), Schaper (+2), Fox (+2), Rai (+4) all have work to do, while England's Tommy Fleetwood also struggled in an opening 76.

Dutchman Luiten, the lowest-ranked player in the field, turned in five under but cancelled two birdies with two bogeys on the back nine to settle for a 67, one clear of Denmark's Jacob Skov Olesen.

#2834. Spurs overcome terrible start to beat Bulls, get 10th straight win

The Spurs' win streak has reached 10 games. Despite looking lackadaisical and digging a hole for themselves early in their visit to Chicago, they managed to mount a second-half comeback to beat the Bulls 124-119.

It's hard to predict when a trap game is coming, but when the Bulls, losers of seven straight heading into the matchup, made the Spurs, winners of nine in a row, look helpless in the first half, it seemed like Wednesday's bout was going to be one of them. The NBA has a way of humbling even the teams that are steamrolling opponents because on any given night, the tables can turn. Early on, the play of De'Aaron Fox and Victor Wembanyama was enough to keep up with a shockingly hot Chicago team that simply couldn't miss, but as the minutes passed, and especially when the bench checked in, San Antonio found itself looking at a mounting deficit and not a lot of answers on how to reduce it.

There were a lot of reasons for the struggles in a first half in which the Silver and Black gave up 70 points. The Bulls' five-out look, with Jalen Smith at center, prevented Wemby from being the defensive presence he normally is at the rim. Some questionable lineups from Mitch Johnson predictably struggled on both ends, with the ones featuring Luke Kornet really floundering, as the big man couldn't make an impact in any area. Fox and Wembanyama did well, but the offense as a whole featured either a lot of hesitation or guys forcing the issue and trying to score one-on-one instead of making a terrible Chicago defense move. It was the perfect storm of poor play from the Spurs and surprisingly good minutes from their opponent.

Trap games will happen, and they normally don't mean much, so when the Silver and Black were down 22 in the second quarter, it was reasonable to assume their streak might end sooner than it should have, and that was fine. As they trimmed the Bulls' lead to 14 heading into the break, a comeback appeared possible, but it seemed like it would require adjustments in strategy and attitude that, after such an underwhelming start, didn't look all that likely. Then, as the second half started, the Spurs decided that, actually, they wanted their 10th win in a row, and that was that.

The ball started moving, and there was no hesitation going to the basket. Harper came alive, both dishing out assists and scoring, while Wemby continued to dominate, roaming more on defense and consistently making the right play on offense. Even the bench, which had been dreadful earlier, held its own thanks to an active Kornet and good minutes from the three forwards.

The Spurs looked like the juggernaut they are in the third, as they tend to do when they don't make unforced errors and play with energy, instead of the timid team that opened the game. It helped that the Bulls came back to earth after a scorching first half from beyond the arc, but the opponent rarely matters when San Antonio starts operating near its highest level. Heading into the final frame, the visitors led by one.

The Spurs only got the lead to 15 at a point in the fourth quarter, which is hardly large enough in the modern NBA to be confident about it being secure, but while the box score didn't accurately reflect how in control San Antonio was, it was obvious to those watching. After showing the low floor that has Mitch Johnson worried in the first half, the Silver and Black had their sky-high ceiling in full display in the second. Consistency is still not the team's strong suit, but as long as they keep winning, no one should really be too worried about the ups and downs of November games.

Wembanyama has been Tim Duncan-esque in the way he doesn't need to have loud games to have great ones. There were spectacular moments involving him, as they always are, but without looking otherworldly, he easily logged 32 points, nine rebounds, and five blocks. It's too early in his career to start taking his greatness for granted, but he has been making the game look so easy lately that it might start happening if we are not careful.

De'Aaron Fox played the perfect Robin to Wemby's Batman in the first half as a scorer, dropping 17 points. Dylan Harper took over the role in the second half, scoring 11 points of his total 18. Harper finished with 10 assists while Fox had nine. On a night in which Stephon Castle struggled, the other starting guards stepped up.

With Devin Vassell still out, Julian Champagnie got another start and had a decent game, finishing with 12 points, two assists, and two steals on 35 minutes of play. Curiously, he didn't log a rebound. It's the second time it has happened this season. Last season, it didn't happen a single time in 82 games. Rebounding is one of Champagnie's strengths, but the different roles and lineups might be affecting his ability to secure boards.

Like most of the team, the bench was bad in the first half, but had a much better showing in the second half, with 21 of the 30 points scored by the non-garbage time subs coming in the last two quarters. Keldon Johnson, who is looking like a much better passer, also had four assists. Everyone did their job, which helped the Spurs tremendously.

The Warriors have been better than expected this season, but Stephen Curry had to leave the game on Wednesday against the Rockets with an injury, so they could be missing their engine in their visit to San Antonio.

#2830. Rockets G Marcus Smart ejected after throwing punch at Warriors' Charles Bassey

Marcus Smart missed the fourth quarter of the Houston Rockets' game against the Golden State Warriors. The NBA might decide he needs to miss even more time.

The veteran guard was ejected on a Flagrant 2 foul after throwing a punch at Warriors center Charles Bassey in the final minutes of the third quarter. The dispute appeared to be instigated when the two players got tangled up on a rebound, with Smart throwing the punch moments later.

Smart clearly knew what was coming after the punch, as he immediately walked off the court rather than wait to hear the officials' ruling. Judging from the replay, it's unclear if the punch even made contact.

Per Matt Young of the Houston Chronicle, Rockets head coach Ime Udoka said after the game that Smart confronted Bassey about wrapping up his elbow on the rebound, to which Bassey replied, "What you gonna do?" Smart responded with the punch.

After the game, Bassey called Smart's punch "fake toughness."

"He's been playing dirty the whole game," Bassey said, according to Yahoo Sports' Kelly Iko. "I don't think he was trying to play, I think he was trying to get kicked out of the game."

Not only was Smart ejected, he can also expect a mandatory fine for the flagrant foul as well as a likely suspension from the NBA. The league's rulebook is fairly clear on that matter:

Any player who throws a punch, whether it connects or not, has committed an unsportsmanlike act. He will be ejected following confirmation during review by instant replay and suspended for a minimum of one game.

Smart exited the game with 6 points on 2-for-2 shooting (both 3-pointers), 2 rebounds, 1 assist, 1 turnover and 4 personal fouls in 17 minutes. His early exit didn't prevent the Rockets from winning 115-108, in a game that also saw the Warriors lose Stephen Curry to an injury in the first quarter.

#2828. Mike Tomlin highlights 12 top coaching candidates for 2027 NFL offseason

The 2026 NFL season has reached its midpoint, and all 32 coaches who began the season in charge of their respective franchises remain in place.

As every NFL fan knows, that will not last long. Last season, 10 of the league's 32 teams made coaching changes. NFL owners are growing ever-impatient as they look to quickly build Super Bowl contenders, so leashes are shorter than ever for coaches.

That's why many will be on scorching hot seats during the second half of the season. And while none have been dismissed yet, a couple may fail to finish out the season, while numerous others will see their tenures end on "Black Monday," the day after the final game of the regular season.

But who will NFL owners and decision-makers be eyeing among the league's next crop of top coaching candidates? A few prominent retreads are on the market, along with the usual plethora of young, offensive-minded coaches.

Here's a look at the top coaching candidates to watch as the 2027 NFL coach hiring cycle draws ever nearer.

Week 3: Denver Broncos cheerleaders perform during the "Sunday Night Football" game against the Los Angeles Rams at Empower Field at Mile High.

Tomlin is easily the biggest name on the NFL's coaching carousel for 2027. The 54-year-old led the Steelers to a 193-114-2 record across 19 seasons in Pittsburgh and won a Super Bowl while in charge of the franchise. He famously never had a losing season, and his steady performance will certainly endear him to NFL organizations looking for a proven culture-builder to improve their operations.

The lone question is whether Tomlin will want to return to the sideline after stepping down from the Steelers following the 2025 NFL season. He is currently working as a studio analyst for NBC and could be happy to remain in that role moving forward.

Unlike Tomlin, McDermott's intentions are known. The 52-year-old wants another crack at being an NFL head coach after leading the Bills to a 98-50 regular-season record across nine seasons in Buffalo.

McDermott is a strong, defensive-minded coach and has shown an ability to bring in quality offensive coordinators, with Joe Brady being among the best hires of his tenure. His track record leading the Bills to consistent success – eight playoff appearances in nine seasons – should appeal to NFL owners, even though McDermott was never quite able to guide any of his Bills teams to the Super Bowl.

McDermott will also be helped in the interview process by the fact that he is not currently employed by an NFL team. That will allow any team that fires their coach in-season to immediately check in with McDermott and gauge where he stands among the top candidates on the market.

The Vikings started the 2026 NFL season with a 6-2 record despite sporting an offense that ranks 30th in EPA per play, per the NFL's Next Gen Stats. Their success has largely been thanks to Flores' defense, which has established itself as the league's best.

Flores' aggressive, blitz-heavy attack has helped Minnesota post the NFL's best pressure rate while ranking second league-wide in defensive EPA per play behind only the Tampa Bay Buccaneers. The Vikings are surrendering a league-low 14.5 points per game and proved, by containing Josh Allen on "Monday Night Football" in Week 9, that they can be a championship-caliber defense.

With that in mind, any NFL organization looking to improve its defense should be interested in bringing Flores aboard. His system has proven transferable across all of his NFL stops, so he is practically guaranteed to turn whatever team he coaches into a top-10 defense.

That said, Flores is still embroiled in a discrimination lawsuit against the NFL, and it isn't clear whether that legal process will make any teams apprehensive about hiring him.

Udinski is just 30 years old, but he's a rising star in the coaching ranks. He has helped the Jaguars become one of the NFL's best and most efficient offenses during the 2026 season, and his ability to scheme players open should make him a hot commodity on the coaching market.

Udinski would be one of the NFL's youngest-ever coaches, but NFL owners have been more willing than ever to follow the Sean McVay blueprint and take chances on young coaches. That could give Udinski a path toward earning a coaching job even with just seven years of NFL coaching experience.

LERNER: He drew up plays at 5. At 30, Jaguars OC Grant Udinski is one of the NFL's hottest coaches

Doyle is in a similar boat to Udinski. He's just 30 years old but has enjoyed a rapid rise over the last two seasons, finding success working as Ben Johnson's offensive coordinator in 2025 before getting a chance to call his own plays with the Ravens this season.

Doyle's ties to Johnson, who has rapidly turned the Bears into a Super Bowl contender, should make him a popular interview during the 2027 coach hiring cycle. He could also improve his candidacy if he's able to effectively lead the Ravens through injuries to quarterbacks Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley and give Baltimore a chance to make a playoff push with Jacoby Brissett at quarterback.

Brothers Matt LaFleur and Mike LaFleur are both NFL coaches. Could Klay Kubiak and Klint Kubiak be next? The latter Kubiak brother has performed well in his first season with the Las Vegas Raiders while the former has been a part of Kyle Shanahan's staff for six seasons.

Kubiak has performed well since being elevated to offensive coordinator in 2025. He led the 49ers to a top-10 offense in both scoring and yardage in his first season in the role and is on pace to do the same in 2026. He has helped Shanahan guide San Francisco to success amid a plethora of injuries at receiver while Brock Purdy has thrived under his tutelage, completing 69.2% of his passes for 4,147 yards, 37 touchdowns and 15 interceptions in 17 starts.

Purdy's success should be enough to get Kubiak interviews; and if NFL teams get the sense he can develop into a quarterback whisperer, they may pull the trigger on hiring him.

Many believed Scheelhaase might earn a coaching job during last year's hiring cycle. At the time, he was coming off a successful season as the Rams' pass game coordinator and had helped lead Matthew Stafford to an MVP season.

Scheelhaase wasn't ultimately hired, but he earned a promotion to the Rams' offensive coordinator role. The Rams have gotten off to a disappointing 5-4 start, but their offense still ranks fourth in yards per game and ninth in EPA per play, per the NFL's Next Gen Stats. That, plus his ties to Sean McVay, will be enough to get Scheelhaase more looks during the 2027 cycle.

It's also worth noting Scheelhaase was a college quarterback at Illinois, so he has experience playing the position. That could prove valuable for teams looking for a coach who can develop a young quarterback into a star-caliber player.

Scheelhaase isn't the only Rams assistant who should be a hot commodity. Shula got plenty of interviews last season after leading the Rams to a top-10 mark in defensive EPA per play and has only continued to raise his stock in 2026.

The Rams presently rank fourth in defensive EPA per play and have allowed the sixth-fewest yards and eighth-fewest points this season. Shula has achieved those results despite missing star edge rusher Myles Garrett for most of the season, and he has helped develop players like Nate Landman into key pieces of the team's defense.

Also, for those wondering, yes: Chris Shula is related to Don Shula. He is the grandson of the legendary Miami Dolphins coach.

Durde has spent the last three seasons as Seattle's defensive coordinator under Mike Macdonald. He has helped Macdonald turn the Seahawks into one of the league's best defenses amid their run to a Super Bowl 60 win over the New England Patriots.

The Seahawks had the NFL's top-scoring defense last season and have allowed the fewest yards thus far in 2026. That will help Durde get looks during this coaching cycle, and so will his and Macdonald's abilities to coach up their young players – like former undrafted free agents Ty Okada and Drake Thomas – and turn them into key role players.

Campanile has spent the last two seasons as the Jaguars' defensive coordinator. He has created a defensive identity in Jacksonville, one focused on attacking downhill and stopping the run. That has paid dividends with the Jaguars allowing just 89.3 rushing yards per game in 2026, fifth-fewest in the league.

NFL owners looking for a hard-nosed, defensive-minded leader could find themselves gravitating toward Campanile. His aggressive defensive style figures to transfer well to his next landing spot, provided he has the personnel needed to stop the run.

The Falcons rank sixth in pressure rate during the 2026 NFL season. This is despite their top pressure-generators being the little-known duo of Brandon Dorlus (29) and Cameron Thomas (26).

Those pressure numbers are a feather-in-the-cap of Ulbrich, who has done well to help an undermanned unit perform at a higher-than-expected level. That's not surprising, given the 49-year-old frequently got the most out of the defenses he coached with the Jets as part of Robert Saleh's staff from 2021-24.

All told, there's a reason the Falcons were adamant about keeping Ulbrich as their defensive coordinator even after switching coaches following the 2026 season. His motivational skills are strong, and his ability to develop defensive talent should earn him interviews this offseason.

Graham has spent eight seasons as an NFL defensive coordinator for four different teams. He is finally enjoying high-end success with the Steelers, who have helped lead to a top-seven mark in yards allowed, points allowed and defensive EPA per play.

Graham, 47, has earned several head coaching interviews during his career and figures to once again draw interest this offseason. His experience across six different NFL organizations and a Super Bowl ring with the Patriots as a defensive assistant could eventually be enough to get him his first chance to be an NFL head coach.

#2825. Yaxel Lendeborg, Warriors can’t overcome Steph Curry injury in loss to Rockets

The Golden State Warriors eight-game winning streak came to an end on Wednesday night, falling 115-108 to the Houston Rockets. While the Dubs will be disappointed to start a difficult five-game roadtrip with a loss, the biggest headline coming out of the contest is the status of Steph Curry. Curry left the game after just a few minutes and several hard falls with "right side soreness" and was quickly ruled out for the game. The organization will hold its breath hoping that Steph will not be unavailable for long.

The Rockets ferocious defense, combining an arguably unparalleled combination of strength, athleticism, length, and physicality throughout their rotation, was already causing Golden State trouble before Curry left the game. Things got even more challenging for the Dubs without the franchise's icon.

Rookie Yaxel Lendeborg continued to impress despite arguably his worst shooting performance of the season (missing two open threes badly and going 5-for-8 from the free-throw line). Even with the added defensive attention coming his way with Curry sidelined, Lendeborg scored 18 points (14 in the second quarter) in the first half. Lendeborg finished with 25 points, 10 rebounds, and 2 steals with just 2 turnovers in 36 minutes.

Lendeborg led the charge as the Warriors chipped away at a 15-point deficit, tying things up at 51 late in teh second quarter before the Rockets closed the half on a 5-0 run.

De'Anthony Melton caught fire for a couple threes in the third quarter to help keep the Dubs within striking distance. Brandin Podziemski was easily the Dubs second-best scorer of the night, scoring 22 points on 7-for-15 shooting from the field, including a four-point play early in the fourth quarter. De'Anthony Melton and LJ Cryer both made a trio of threes and finished with 24 combined points, but Golden State needed another strong offensive performance in Steph's absence that ultimately never came.

As Houston seemed to finally be pulling away in the third quarter, particularly while Lendeborg got a brief break on the bench, Rockets guard Marcus Smart inexplicably through a punch at Warriors center Charles Bassey, getting ejected from the game and slowing his team's momentum. Heading into the fourth quarter, the Warriors were not out of the game yet.

The Warriors were able to once again cut the deficit down to three in the final quarter, but the Rockets once again responded with an 8-0 run. Golden State simply lacked the firepower to keep up with Houston. As head coach Steve Kerr asked for more prolonged minutes from his remaining healthy players, they seemed to run out of gas. Another Rockets run put them ahead by 18 points with three minutes to go and Kerr pulled the plug.

Every member of the Rockets starting lineup scored at least 14 points and Reed Sheppard added 13 off the bench. Kevin Durant led the Rockets in scoring with 20. Alperen Şengün recorded 19 points, 13 rebounds, and 9 assists. Fred Van Vleet had 18 points, 6 assists, 3 steals, and 2 rebounds on 6-for-9 shooting from the field (4-for-7 from three) and finished with a game-best +25 plus/minus.

The Warriors will have one day off before they face Victor Wembanyama and the San Antonio Spurs on Friday night. Given how quickly Curry was ruled out tonight, it seems unlikely he will play. Nevertheless, the Warriors will presumably have a sense of how long he'll be out in the coming days.

#2823. Would Yhonzae Pierre fit in the Panthers new defensive scheme next season?

The 2027 NFL Draft feels like forever away, and we're all caught up in the start of the NFL season. Regardless, it's never too early to look ahead to the Draft. This weekly series will take a closer look at some of the prospects the Carolina Panthers could select in the 2027 iteration of the Draft. In this series, we'll only be looking at prospects the Panthers could seriously consider. Today our profile will focus on Alabama Crimson Tide edge defender Yhonzae Pierre.

Pierre (6'3", 251 lbs.) used his status as a four star recruit in Alabama to earn an All-American Bowl invitation. Despite being offered scholarships to several SEC powerhouses, Pierre chose the home state Crimson Tide. Pierre spent his freshman season in 2023 redshirted before becoming a rotational piece in 2024. While not expected to start last season, injuries to Alabama's top edges gave Pierre an opportunity, and he never looked back. He ended up leading Alabama with 14.5 tackles for loss and eight sacks. He returned for this year and switched to number zero, taking over Deontae Lawson's legacy. Pierre has been a bit banged up this year but has 23 tackles with 5.5 for loss and 4.5 sacks through nine games. He even added a pick six for good measure.

Pierre is a pass rushing producer, again leading Alabama in pressures with 25 so far this season. He plays the run with strength and a solid anchor, holding the point of attack well for his size. Pierre also has a knack for taking over certain games, including six pressures against Florida State this year. He's able to use his above average athletic ability and strength to win on the outside, making a concerted effort this season of getting his hands up when his pass rush stalls (he has four passes deflected already this year).

While his pressure numbers have stayed steady, his inconsistent tackling leads to him not finishing some plays. He also loses his pad level on longer pass rush reps, allowing the offensive lineman to get him off balance. While Pierre is not a small player, he doesn't have the elite edge defender height and weight, which could affect how he fits into some NFL defensive schemes.

Pierre is a hard working, talented edge rusher. He uses his quick start and power to perform well against the run and the pass. He's a bit hot and cold, with his pressures coming in bunches, but the pass rush prowess is undeniable. Pierre's ceiling is viewed as a high-end starter if he can finish better at the quarterback. He has plenty of pressures, but his counters are still developing allowing opposing quarterbacks to escape too often. With the Panthers struggling to get to the quarterback, taking an edge rusher isn't out of the question. The problem with projecting who they might take, however, is not knowing who the long term defensive coordinator will be and what scheme they'll implement. A 3-4 DC would likely be more open to someone like Pierre, whereas a base 4-3 coach might want someone a bit larger.

What do you think, Panthers fans? If Yhonzae Pierre is available when the Panthers select in the 2027 NFL Draft, should they take him? Sound off in the comments!

#2818. YouTube Gold: Cooper Flagg Is Well On His Way To Greatness

Former Duke star Cooper Flagg is now in his second NBA season, and so far, he's just crushing it. He's still 19 – he won't turn 20 until next month – and he's not playing like a normal young player.

He's moving closer to an elite level. Flagg is averaging 25.9 points, 6.9 rebounds, 6.9 assists, 2.2 steals, and just under a block per game.

Last year, you'll remember, he hit 51 points on April 3 against fellow former Dukie Paolo Banchero.

As a rookie, like most rookies, it took him some time to settle into the pace of the game. He appears to have done so now, and the question is: what's his ceiling?

He still needs to improve his three-point shooting, but you can't just foul him: he's hitting 90.3 percent from the line.

More importantly, as this video discusses, he's learning to use his team as an instrument on offense. Lots of guys can score tons of points, but relatively few learn to see their team that way.

He wasn't a basketball player, but Wayne Gretzky did that in hockey. He could see where the puck needed to be and got it a pass or two away from being there.

Flagg is doing something similar for Dallas, but he's also playing brilliantly on defense. He's a superb weapon on both ends of the court.

Add it all up, and the Mavericks have an immensely promising player on their hands, and he's still a good ways from his full potential.

#2813. Michigan State Receives Major Injury News Ahead of Critical Washington Showdown

Michigan State football received some encouraging injury news Wednesday night, but Pat Fitzgerald's Spartans aren't out of the woods just yet. With a critical Big Ten showdown against Washington approaching, several important players are trending in the right direction while others have already been ruled out.

According to Chris Solari of the Detroit Free Press, defensive tackle Ben Roberts, cornerbacks Tre Bell and Charles Brantley, and backup quarterback Cam Fancher have all been upgraded to questionable for Saturday's matchup.

That's a potentially significant development for a Michigan State team that has been battered by injuries throughout the season.

Michigan State (55) Ben Roberts shows off the Paul Bunyan trophy with his teammates to fans after their 15-6 win overt Michigan at Michigan Stadium in Ann Arbor on Saturday, Nov. 7, 2026. Credit: USA TODAY Network via Reuters Connect (Reuters)

The most encouraging name on the injury report might be Roberts, who suffered a leg injury against UCLA on October 24 and had to leave the field in a wheelchair. The standout defensive tackle missed Michigan State's stunning 15-6 victory over Michigan last weekend, but his upgrade to questionable raises the possibility of a return.

Getting Bell and Brantley back would also provide a much-needed boost to Michigan State's secondary. Both cornerbacks have missed the last three games, forcing the Spartans to rely on their depth.

Running back Jaziun Patterson was also upgraded from out to doubtful, although his availability remains uncertain. As Matt Wenzel of MLive reported, Michigan State could welcome back several important contributors for its first home game in a month.

Despite the encouraging developments, Michigan State has already ruled out 10 players for Saturday's game, including leading tackler Devin Vaught and starting wide receiver Rodney Bullard Jr. Vaught recorded an interception against Michigan before suffering another injury, making his absence particularly disappointing.

The Spartans will also be without linebacker Jordan Hall, tight end Jayden Savoury and running backs Marvis Parrish and Cam Edwards. Additional absences include Brayden Thomas, Jay Coyne, Tristan Comer and Joe Caudill.

It's hardly an ideal situation for a Michigan State squad trying to build on its emotional victory over Michigan.

Michigan State enters Saturday's contest at 3-6 overall and 1-5 in Big Ten play, while Washington sits at 5-4. The Spartans must win their final three regular-season games to reach six victories and become bowl eligible.

That makes Saturday's game especially important, as Fitzgerald's team attempts to keep its bowl eligibility hopes alive.

There's also uncertainty on Washington's side, with quarterback Demond Williams Jr. and running back Trey Cooley listed as questionable on the Huskies' initial availability report.

For Michigan State, the immediate priority is getting as many healthy players back as possible without compromising their recovery. The Spartans proved against Michigan that they can overcome adversity, but doing it again Saturday could require another impressive effort.

Michigan State hosts Washington on Saturday, November 14, at 4:15 p.m. ET at Spartan Stadium. The game will air on Big Ten Network.

#2806. Lions preview: Breaking down Patriots’ offensive, defensive schemes

The Detroit Lions (6-2) and New England Patriots (5-4) are headed to Munich, Germany for a Week 10 battle between interconference opponents. Despite coming off a Super Bowl appearance in 2025, the Patriots' season has been wildly inconsistent, looking unbeatable at times and completely vulnerable at others. While it's impossible to know which Patriots team will show up on any given week, the Lions expect New England at their best in this matchup.

"Real good team here," Lions coach Dan Campbell said of the Patriots. "I know the record is not the true indication of what this team is. There's a number of things that go into that. […] You look at the totality of what this team is capable of, the firepower they have, and they're a good team. They're dangerous. And they're going to clean a lot of things up."

Former Patriots quarterback and current color commentator Tom Brady covered the Patriots' last game against the Packers—a 45-27 blowout—and he was less kind in his assessment of the Patriots:

"Half the season they played like this, and the other half of the season they haven't turned the ball over, they haven't exposed the defense, have played more physical on defense, but right now it's just too inconsistent. Which team are they going to get? The team that is loose with the ball, is loose with decision-making, looking like they can be beaten by anybody. Or the team that takes care of the football, that makes the other team play left-handed, and can do what they want to do. Can they be a good team? They haven't quite done that because good teams leave the smallest margin of error."

#2804. What does Steph Curry injury mean for Warriors? Can they weather the storm?

Stephen Curry, the greatest shooter of all-time, walked off the floor Wednesday, Nov. 11 during a game against the Houston Rockets, clearly grimacing. Curry, who appeared to be favoring his right side, then walked to the locker room, with a trainer following. The Golden State Warriors announced minutes into the second quarter that Curry would not return for the rest of the game with right abdominal soreness.

Warriors coach Steve Kerr said after the game that Curry said he "tweaked something in his abdominal area," which Curry told his longtime coach he had never done before. Kerr said Curry left the arena to get an MRI.

"Concern is high but I have no idea the extent of the injury," Kerr said.

This is stating the obvious, but it will depend on the severity.

While Curry was in clear discomfort when he left the floor, he did try to tough it out for a few possessions. That alone is not enough of an indicator to provide a tangible assessment, but it could hint to the degree of severity.

Either way, abdominal or oblique injuries can be complicated. Because they are part of the core muscles, many basic athletic movements can become painful.

They can also take time to heal, especially if there's a muscle tear. Magic forward Paolo Banchero, for example, missed 34 games during the 2024-25 season with a torn oblique. If it ends up being a strain, however, any Curry absence would undoubtedly be far shorter.

Regardless, if Curry is forced to miss time, the Warriors will face the impossible problem of making up for the loss of a player who's irreplaceable.

Curry is playing at an MVP-caliber level, and Golden State simply does not have any options who come close. Rookie Yaxel Lendeborg has been sensational in his first month in the NBA, and surely he will be forced to take on more offensive responsibility. Same goes for starting guard Brandin Podziemski, though to a lesser extent.

What the Warriors won't be able to replace is Curry's perimeter shooting and the way that opens up space on the floor. Curry is the best off-ball player in the NBA and tirelessly bounds around the floor, looking for small gaps. Those alone are enough for him to get shots off.

And, considering he entered the night making field goals at 50.3% and 3-pointers at 45.7%, that efficiency will be sorely missed.

The Warriors haven't issued an update on Jimmy Butler's status, but if Curry is forced to miss extended time, that could potentially put some pressure on Butler to lead the team as soon as he returns, should the timing coincide.

Either way, if Curry misses extended time, it's tough to see Golden State remaining as competitive as it has been to start the season.

Si allunga la lista degli indisponibili della Lazio. Secondo quanto riportato da Il Tempo, Dopo Zaccagni, fermo per l’infortunio rimediato contro il Cagliari, anche Sutalo e Nuno Tavares sono usciti malconci dalla trasferta di Napoli e hanno dovuto rinunciare agli impegni con le rispettive nazionali. #2797. Stephen Curry exits with injury in Warriors’ loss to Rockets: ‘Concern is high’

Something was bound to bring an end to the Warriors' winning streak at some point. It just so happened to be snapped at eight games in the worst possible way Wednesday night.

Stephen Curry left Golden State's 115-108 loss to the Rockets in Houston with what the team is terming an abdominal injury.

About five minutes into the first quarter, he hoisted a 3-pointer, grabbed at his right side, let out a yell and was removed from the game, never to return.

Stephen Curry grimaces as he leaves the court during a timeout in the Warriors' 115-108 loss to the Rockets on Nov. 11, 2026 in Houston. AP Photo/Ashley Landis

The Warriors didn't have many details to share immediately after the loss.

Coach Steve Kerr said that Curry left the Toyota Center in the second half to get an MRI but was still anxiously awaiting the results.

"We haven't heard anything," Kerr said. "Concern is high. But I have no idea the extent of the injury."

Curry went over to Kerr after the Warriors called timeout at the 6:25 mark and passed along a brief message before heading straight into the locker room. He had been moving gingerly before the shot and remained in the game for about a minute after until Kerr was able to sub him out.

"He just said he tweaked something in his abdominal area. Said he's never done it before," Kerr said. "I just saw him holding his side and tried to get him out as quickly as I could."

Golden State head coach Steve Kerr reacts during the Warriors' loss to the Rockets. Getty Images

Curry, 38, was limited to 43 games last season, the third-fewest of his career. He was forced to miss 27 in a row at one point with inflammation in his knee commonly known as runner's knee.

Curry and the Warriors understood they would have to manage his knee this season. They overhauled his offseason training regimen and started him on a tight minutes restriction.

Now, they've been thrown a new wrench with another injury.

Stephen Curry leaves the court during the Warriors' loss to the Rockets. AP Photo/Ashley Landis

Losing Curry for any amount of time amounts to basically the Warriors' worst nightmare, but it stings even more given how he has started his 18th season. Despite averaging only 28.6 minutes per game, Curry is scoring 30.6 per contest, second only to Luka Doncic.

Curry's MVP-level play is one reason Golden State was able to become the first team in NBA history to reel off eight wins in a row after starting a season 0-3. Along with the emergence of rookie Yaxel Lendeborg, it appeared the Warriors could more than tread water before Jimmy Butler and Moses Moody return from their respective knee surgeries in the new year.

Stephen Curry looks on during the first quarter of the Warriors' loss to the Rockets. Getty Images

It will be hard to keep their heads above water if Curry is forced to miss any extended time.

Brandon Williams, Golden State's backup point guard, missed the game in Houston after tweaking his hamstring against the Magic two nights earlier. Will Richard, a second-year shooting guard, is attempting to play through a torn ligament in his thumb.

#2796. Explaining LSU coach Lane Kiffin's history with Steve Sarkisian

LSU football coach Lane Kiffin will see a familiar face on the opposing sideline when LSU meets Texas this week. Kiffin and Longhorns head coach Steve Sarkisian share a deep history, dating back to their time at USC.

Kiffin and Sarkisian first worked together at USC in 2001. They were both on the USC staff from 2001 to 2003, and together in LA again from 2005-06.

"We have a really unique relationship. It goes back a long time … I was a first-year tight end coach. He was a GA actually, coaching the quarterbacks with Norm Chow, and then just kind of a really intertwined, strange thing of kind of growing up in those years and having the great offenses together," Kiffin said.

In 2005, the tandem worked together as Kiffin was the offensive coordinator and Sark was the QB coach. They shared playcalling duties. That unit proved to be one of the best in USC history, averaging 49.1 points and 579 yards per game.

Kiffin would leave USC to accept a job as Raiders' head coach. But a few years later, Kiffin returned to USC as head coach. Kiffin fell short of expectations at USC, and when Kiffin was fired, Sarkisian took over the program.

Kiffin landed on his feet as Alabama's offensive coordinator, where he and Sark would reunite. Alabama hired Sarkisian as an offensive analyst, and when Kiffin left for FAU's head coaching job, Sarkisian took over as Alabama's offensive coordinator.

Kiffin and Sark have met as head coaches three times before, all in the PAC-12 when Sarkisian was at Washington and Kiffin was at USC. Kiffin went 2-1 vs. Sarkisian in that stretch from 2010-12.

#2795. When will Jaxson Dart return? Giants QB suffered leg injury Week 2, timeline

Hard to hit a bullseye without a Dart. But the New York Giants are trying.

Big Blue sits within the playoff picture entering the second half of the season despite missing starting quarterback Jaxson Dart. The second-year quarterback has missed every game since sustaining a knee injury in the Giants' Week 2 loss to the Los Angeles Rams.

In his absence, New York has managed to put together a 4-1-1 record. The defense has held its own thanks to the efforts of second-year Abdul Carter, rookie Arvell Reese and an ascendant secondary. On offense, the Giants' acquisition of quarterback J.J. McCarthy in a late September trade has kept the unit clicking without its usual leader.

At 5-2-1, New York sits in first place in the NFC East and holds a two-game lead over the Dallas Cowboys.

McCarthy is 2-0 as the Giants' starter going into Week 10. Winston remains his backup.

#2793. The Celtics ended their 4-0 road trip in perfect fashion

TORONTO — Mitchell Robinson couldn't help himself.

The Celtics center chirped both Derrick White and Neemias Queta during their postgame scrums after the Celtics erased a 22-point deficit to win their fifth straight game, a 98-93 victory over the Toronto Raptors.

"You the fucking man!" he yelled to White after his 19-point, 4-block performance.

"Way to knock down those free throws," he said to Queta after the Portuguese big man hit two free throws with 15 seconds to spare, giving the Celtics a three-point lead.

After a week-long road trip, the Celtics' plane heading back to Boston was delayed, but locker-room vibes remained exceptionally high.

Players are usually quick to head out after the last game of a long road trip, but the flight delay kept nearly everyone in their lockers, bantering for far longer than normal.

Wednesday's win, after all, was a full-team effort worth celebrating. Ron Harper Jr. blasted Olivia Dean on the aux. Robinson requested the team play some country music. Payton Pritchard threw out song requests.

"He's the guy," Queta said, referring to Harper Jr.'s DJing.

In a way, it was only fitting that Harper, one of just a handful of players who never checked into the ball game, was directing the postgame jam sesh.

"Even the guys that weren't playing just gave us a lift, energy-wise," Joe Mazzulla said after the game.

The Celtics went 4-0 on the trip, beating the Charlotte Hornets, Milwaukee Bucks, Philadelphia 76ers, and the Raptors. After dropping their Opening Day game against the Detroit Pistons, they have won 9 of their last 10 games. They have the league's best net rating. And they're playing 11 guys every night, leaning on as much depth as they can.

Less than 24 hours after blowing out the 76ers, they found themselves on the other side of a blowout, in danger of ending the trip on a sour note.

"We talked about the four-game road trip, and we kind of talked about it before the game," Jayson Tatum said. "Last night was a good win against a really good team, and last night wouldn't mean anything — because it was easy to get up for a game like yesterday. Can we muster up some energy to will ourselves to a victory today?"

The quick turnaround meant an 8 pm game followed by a 7 pm tip, with a border crossing in between. And while Tuesday's game against the 76ers may have had a playoff atmosphere, Wednesday's was against a .500 team missing its two best players in Scottie Barnes and Kawhi Leonard.

"Obviously anybody could get up for a game like yesterday," White said. "That's what you dream about, stuff like that, especially the atmosphere and the energy around the building. But then you come on the road, and Toronto is a really good team. Obviously they're missing a lot of players, but they've done a lot of great things in the league, so it's kind of a little letdown. And so that's where you've got to find that extra gear."

Tatum noted that the team got to their Toronto hotel at 3 a.m.

"I'd be lying if I said I wasn't tired today," he said.

But, that made the outcome of Wednesday's game all the more important.

"If you want to be great, these are games that you have to [win]," he said.

The Raptors shot 10-for-18 from three in the first quarter and led by as many as 22 in the second.

"Anytime they hit 10 threes in a quarter, it seems like it's going to be a long night," White said. "But honestly, we were just kind of a step behind."

Mazzulla said the game turned in the second quarter, as the Celtics chipped away at the deficit to seven before halftime. The Celtics embarked on a 17-2 run, of which Jayson Tatum scored and assisted on 14 points.

"We didn't win the game in the fourth quarter. We won the game in the middle of the second quarter when we cut it to seven," Mazzulla said. "It's a 22-point game. It could go either way. You can kind of mail it in and just say, 'Second night of a back-to-back, blah, blah, blah.'"

Tatum finished with 24 points, 8 assists, 7 rebounds, and 2 blocks. White added 19 points, 7 assists, and 4 blocks.

And Queta, who led the game with 13 rebounds, went out of his way to shout out the small plays that don't show up in a box score.

"A lot of guys are making energy plays that galvanize your teammates, and I think we kind of fed off each other," Queta said. "I think that's something we need to do more, and it will get us over the hump on those nights."

Defense became the name of the game; Boston held Toronto to 19 points in the third quarter and 13 in the fourth.

"I thought all five guys were connected defensively," Mazzulla said. "We were executing our coverages, forced the type of shots that we wanted them to shoot. Guys got rebounds. So I thought it was a connected defensive effort by our guys, particularly in the second half."

Mazzulla also praised Queta for his all-around impact on the ballgame.

"Those two big free throws that he hit down the stretch, catching the ball in the seam, making plays," Mazzulla said. "Defensively, he just lifts us to another level with the rim protection."

The Celtics could have simply accepted they didn't have it on Wednesday night.

Instead, they dug deep and finished a successful road trip on top.

"We had every excuse in the world, and people would be like, 'Oh, it was a big game,' whatever," White said. "But it's just the DNA. We put that Celtics uniform on and go out there and win. And so every person that came in there — we might not have had it at the beginning of the game, but we just found a way to find something and give everything we've got."

#2792. Why Columbus, Philadelphia & Portland are MLS Cup dark horses

The Audi 2026 MLS Cup Playoffs are here, with eye-catching matchups across the Eastern Conference and Western Conference.

We've heard all season about clubs that have established themselves as favorites, and about superstar players who can lead their teams to glory.

But instead of focusing on those squads, today we're diving into three dark horse candidates who could make a deep run during the 2026 playoffs.

⚽ 🏆 Think You've Got the Winning Bracket?!

The Audi 2026 MLS Cup Playoffs Bracket Challenge is now open. Make your picks, build your bracket, and compete for your chance to win.

The Columbus Crew are the most in-form team in MLS. They're red-hot at the right time, with six straight wins to end the regular season, and Laurent Courtois has implemented a similar style of play that made the Crew a consistent contender under Wilfried Nancy.

But what's truly made the difference in Columbus' incredible run is the players they signed during the summer transfer window. Brais Méndez (6g/4a) has been elite in midfield, Santi Rodríguez (3g/8a) offers another strong creative outlet, and Gonzalo Tapia has been a goalscoring machine with 6g/2a in just six starts.

Full credit to the front office for bringing in the right pieces and not giving up on the season when a shot at reaching the playoffs looked unlikely at best earlier this year.

I also want to shout out Taha Habroune and Dylan Chambost. Habroune, for me, is the best young player in MLS that no one talks about. He is so underappreciated and brings tactical flexibility that makes him lethal. Chambost has given the Crew a creative element deeper in midfield. He's good technically and in possession and has upgraded his final product this year, especially lately, with five assists in his last four games. With all these pieces clicking, it's no surprise Columbus turned it on as their campaign wound down.

Their Round One opponent is Inter Miami, and I think it's a perfect matchup for the Crew to spring an upset. Columbus' attack is firing on all cylinders right now, while the Herons' defense has been a major pain point all season. The Crew aren't afraid to dominate possession and have plenty of attackers who can break down Miami's beleaguered backline. Columbus will be the underdog, and beating Lionel Messi and Miami in the playoffs is no easy task… but I can tell you from experience, they're not infallible. The Crew have a real shot.

The Philadelphia Union played their final match before the 2026 FIFA World Cup, falling 6-4 to Inter Miami CF. The loss ensured they'd enter the break last in the East (seven points) and coincided with the club parting ways with 2025 Sigi Schmid Coach of the Year winner Bradley Carnell.

Enter interim coach Ryan Richter, who's hit an absolute home run since taking over.

He's shored up the back line, bringing youngster Neil Pierre back from loan and star defender Kai Wagner back from Birmingham City. He made offensive adjustments, pairing Milan Iloski up front with Bruno Damiani, which has led to a breakout second half of the season for both players. And of course, the biggest move of all was giving the keys to the team over to teenage sensation Cavan Sullivan.

Richter's coaching decisions seem to have paid off in spades. Pierre is now a USMNT center back, Iloski ended the season fifth in the Golden Boot presented by Audi race (19g/7a), Damiani scored 13 goals (12 after the World Cup break), and Sullivan has emerged as one of the league's top players in the latter half of the season with 8g/11a post-World Cup. Look at how that compares to Lionel Messi (14g/9a) and Antoine Griezmann (14/8a) over that span! Even Quinn Sullivan, who returned from an ACL injury earlier this year, has elevated his game.

All in all, the Union have put together one of the best turnarounds in recent memory in 2026. Under Richter, they've returned to their Philly roots. They press high, get numbers in the box, and have some of the league's best chance-creation numbers. They've taken 43 of a possible 57 points since Richter took charge, and they'll be an extremely tough out for the New England Revolution in Round One.

Like Columbus, it became really hard to bet against the Portland Timbers down the stretch this season. They're hotter than anyone in the West right now with four consecutive wins, and head coach Martí Cifuentes has really started to get the best out of his squad, specifically his front four.

It's a front four with a lot of expectations and they've answered the call this year. Cifuentes has unlocked David Da Costa (7g/11a) in a way so many Timbers fans were hoping for; his play in the final third has greatly improved. Meanwhile, Vincent Janssen has added an immediate goalscoring boost to help soothe the loss of Kevin Kelsy in the summer, and Ari Lassiter (8g/7a) is having the best season of his lengthy MLS career.

But the standout has been DP winger Kristoffer Velde. The Norwegian is in the best form of his Portland career, leading the team with 22 goal contributions (12g/10a) and is entering the postseason as perhaps the league's hottest player, with four goals in his last three games.

It's also worth noting that in their final four regular-season matches, the Timbers defeated a Western Conference contender in St. Louis, as well as two solid playoff teams in Houston and Charlotte. This team has what it takes to take down top-tier opposition.

The biggest question mark for Portland will be their defense. Joel Waterman has come in and provided stability at center back with a veteran presence, as has Ronald Hernández, who's quietly been a great signing and helped solidify things as a defense-first outside back.

We know what Portland can do in attack. Defeating FC Dallas, with Petar Musa – MLS's most impactful No. 9 – leading the line, will be no small task in Round One. But if the Timbers can put it all together defensively, they could be a real threat for a deep postseason run in the West.

#2784. Dolphins QB Kyle McCord: Experience in big games something I can draw from

It will also be McCord's first regular season action and first game snaps of any kind with the Dolphins. The 2025 sixth-round pick spent last season on the Eagles' practice squad and spent the summer with the Packers before being traded to Miami at the end of August.

It's not my first time I'm playing in a big game. So yeah, I feel like that's where I'm drawing a lot of confidence from."

McCord would not be doing himself any favors by making more of the moment than it is, but that first play call and first snap against NFL opposition will likely lead to a quick realization that it's a different world from his last time in the saddle for a meaningful game.

#2783. Luka Doncic’s, Austin Reaves’ injury scares dose of scary deja vu for Lakers

In what was supposed to be a measuring stick game against the Thunder, Luka Dončić suffered a strained hamstring that sidelined him the entire postseason and Austin Reaves sustained a strained oblique that kept him out the first four games of the playoffs.

Just like that, the Lakers were wiped off the board.

Their two leading scorers were taken out amid a 16-2 run. Their chances of winning a championship were gone. It was over for the purple and gold.

So, when Dončić and Reaves both suffered injuries in a 123-112 win over the Bucks, it was hard not to wince.

Luckily for the Lakers, this was not that.

Dončić said "I'm good" following falling to the floor and clutching his right ankle after Kyle Kuzma closed out on him as he made a 23-foot jumper in the fourth quarter.

His status is more uncertain after he suffered a left heel injury in the third quarter, subbed himself out three minutes later and didn't return. Lakers coach JJ Redick said his X-rays were negative and that he will get imaging on Thursday. He added that his Achilles' tendon is not a concern.

Austin Reaves, who exited with a left heel injury, drives on Jericho Sims during the Lakers' win over the Bucks on Nov. 11, 2026, in Milwaukee. Getty Images

Luckily, this doesn't seem catastrophic for a Laker team that has the best record in the league at 11-2 and has won seven straight games. At least this time, both of their superstars weren't wiped out in the same game.

But it was scary. It was a dose of deja vu. It was a reminder of how fragile all of this is.

The Lakers have been the greatest surprise of the league this season.

No one thought they'd be championship contenders. Not after LeBron James left. Not after they acquired nine new players. Not after there were questions around Walker Kessler after he missed all but five games last season because of shoulder surgery.

The assumptions were unanimous: The Lakers needed time to develop chemistry. And they were still a piece or two away from competing, given their thinness at backup center and the question marks around their perimeter defense.

But those concerns proved to be nothing but white noise.

Luka Dončić, who suffered a minor ankle injury, smiles during the Lakers' win over the Bucks AP Photo/Aaron Gash

Dončić is having an MVP-caliber season, leading the league in scoring with 39.3 points, 7.8 rebounds and 7.9 assists per game.

Reaves is putting on a MasterClass in efficiency with 25.3 points on 50.8% shooting from the field and 41.3% from beyond the arc.

Together, Dončić and Reaves are the league's highest-scoring duo by a landslide, averaging 64.6 points — 12 points ahead of the No. 2 duo of Steph Curry and Yaxel Lendeborg, who are averaging 52 points.

Kessler is a double-double machine, averaging 14.9 points and 10.8 rebounds a game.

And the Lakers' role players are starring in their roles.

Luka Dončić looks at his ankle during the second half of the Lakers' win over the Bucks. AP Photo/Aaron Gash

That's why it was so jolting when it seemed as though the same thread was yanked from the sweater on Wednesday, threatening to unravel everything.

Dončić and Reaves injured in the same game again? Just 223 days after the April 2 disaster?

It was hard not to fear that amid another stunning streak, they were decapitated again. It was hard not to worry that history was repeating itself.

But Dončić 's injury apparently isn't serious.

"Obviously it's painful a little bit, but I'll be fine," said Dončić , who had 32 points, five rebounds, five assists and three steals in 32 minutes on Wednesday.

And the Lakers only can hope that after what happened to them a short while ago, they'll catch a break with Reaves, too.

If anything, Wednesday was the ultimate reminder of how quickly things can change. It's a lesson they learned a short while ago. It's one that still haunts them.

#2779. 4 key matchups to watch in Bengals vs. Steelers

The Cincinnati Bengals will host the Pittsburgh Steelers in primetime football this week. They face off at Paycor Stadium on Sunday Night Football with first place on the line in the division.

Both teams enter this game at 6-2, with Pittsburgh taking a close win over Cincinnati in Week 3.

Let's look at some of the key matchups to watch in this massive AFC North clash.

Amarius Mims has been playing at an All-Pro level all season long. Mims has allowed just five total pressures through eight regular-season games. He is absolutely locking guys down on his side of the line. He is also left on an island more than about any other tackle in the NFL. While he is starting to get more recognition, a primetime matchup for first place in the division against TJ Watt and the Pittsburgh Steelers could really boost his chances for an All-Pro this season.

Meanwhile, TJ Watt has already surpassed his sack total from the 2025 season. Watt currently has 7.5 sacks through just eight games played. Last season, he recorded seven sacks during the entire season. Watt is still one of the better defenders in the league, but will certainly have his hands full trying to get around Mt. Mims this week. This is great versus great, and should be a must-see matchup.

Tackling Darnell Washington is not a fun task, and the Bengals have been one of the worst teams at trying to do so over the years. Washington had 67 yards against the Bengals during Week 3, including a big rumble down the sideline during the 4th quarter.

There was also a play earlier in the game where Jordan Battle tried to come in high on Washington and make the tackle. It won't be just Battle who has to play well against Washington, but it would be nice to see him learn from his mistakes against him earlier in the season.

As for Jordan Battle, it feels like he has started to play some pretty solid football the past few games. His tackling overall has been much more sound, and he made some huge plays against Tennessee in coverage a couple of weeks ago. This past game against Atlanta was the first game of the season that he was not credited with a single missed tackle.

Hopefully that is a positive sign for this week, because Washington is a BIG test.

Jaylen Warren was incredible in Week 3 against the Bengals, who had a hard time getting him on the ground all day long. Warren had 17 carries for 117 yards rushing. He also added an additional three receptions for another 49 yards. The Bengals will have to keep Warren in check if they want to get the win this week.

Two of the main guys responsible for making sure he doesn't have another game like that will be Demetrius Knight Jr. and Barrett Carter. While both guys have been okay this year, I wouldn't call that Pittsburgh game earlier in the year one of their better games. Both guys will need to have a better showing this week at home against the Steelers.

The Bengals are always going to be a pass-first team, but that doesn't mean that Chase Brown cannot have some big rushing performances. This may need to be one of those weeks. Brown had 13 carries for 61 yards during the first matchup against the Steelers. If the Bengals can get some balance with the run game, it will keep Pittsburgh's pass rush at bay.

Brown has been starting to heat up the last couple of weeks as well, and this could be a breakout performance if given the opportunity. One thing I have appreciated all season is his blocking ability in pass protection. He is starting to get more involved in the passing game and showing more elusiveness as a runner in the open field.

If Chase Brown can get going against Pittsburgh, I think the Bengals will come out of Sunday Night Football sitting atop the AFC North at 7-2.

#2776. Week 10 NFL Survivor strategy and picks

The NFL is headed into the proverbial dog days before the holiday season heats up the playoff race. The trade deadline was this past Tuesday and it was plenty active. The Jaguars were the busiest team, while the Vikings made arguably the most important move, trading for Kirk Cousins after Carson Wentz stunk up the joint on Monday Night Football.

If you were still alive in your Survivor pool headed into Week 9, you probably remain so heading into Week 10. There were very few upsets, with Packers over Patriots being the only betting upset. I took the Bears and it worked out for me, although the Bucs did give them some trouble.

I'm back with my Week 10 Survivor picks ahead of Commanders-Giants on Thursday Night Football. Survivor involves picking one team each week to win. If they win you advance, if they lose you're out in most cases. I'm in one pool with four entries, but it's also a double-elimination pool. I'm down to one entry that has one strike against it, but I continue rolling along.

Below I've got a look at the safest choices, best value picks, and then trap picks. I'll post my own picks at the end. I'll update on Sunday once I lock in my three picks for the weekend.

I've included odds for the favorite in each game, courtesy of FanDuel.

Jaguars (-10.5) over TitansRams (-9.5) over CardinalsBills (-8) over Jets

But now that we're past the halfway point, you can't necessarily be as picky. You'll likely want to save some of these squads, but if your pool is getting light, these are all good picks.

#2774. Warriors center calls out Marcus Smart’s ‘fake toughness’ after punch leads to ejection

Marcus Smart took a swing at Golden State's Charles Bassey and got himself ejected from the Warriors' 115-108 loss to the Rockets on Wednesday night in Houston.

The Warriors center saved his punches for after the game.

"Obviously," Bassey said, "that was weak."

Houston guard Marcus Smart (center) is held back after a confrontation with Golden State center Charles Bassey (left) during the Warriors' 115-108 road loss to the Rockets on Nov. 11, 2026. AP Photo/Ashley Landis

Bassey and Smart got tangled up going for a rebound on a Warriors offensive possession late in the third quarter. They continued the spat down to the opposite end of the court, and Smart eventually turned and put his chest into Bassey. Then, he took a step back and threw a punch.

Bassey kept his arms by his side during the whole exchange.

"I just think that, honestly, that was fake toughness," Bassey said. "It didn't surprise me. I knew he was going to do something. … I don't think he was trying to play. I think he was trying to get himself out of the game. If he was trying to play, he wouldn't do that. I think he was trying to chill. He wasn't trying to hoop, bro. That was fake — fake toughness."

Charles Bassey defends Alperen Sengun during the Warriors' loss to the Rockets on Nov. 11, 2026, in Houston. NBAE via Getty Images

According to Bassey's account, he was just trying to grab an offensive rebound when Smart took hold of his hand. Even as the game shifted to the other end of the court, he wouldn't let go.

Earlier, Bassey said, Smart grabbed his knee while setting a screen.

"He's been playing dirty the whole game, man," Bassey said.

Golden State's Charles Bassey arrives before the Warriors' win over the Magic on Nov. 7, 2026, at Chase Center in San Francisco. NBAE via Getty Images

As they were going back and forth down the court, Bassey said, "He said, 'Hey, don't do that.' And I said, 'Do what?' Like, it's a grown-ass league, you know? But he was trying to chill tonight."

#2773. Duke Football: Miami Game Preview

Duke (7-2, 4-2) travels to Coral Gables to take on Miami (8-1, 6-0) on Saturday at 12:00 p.m. EST. Television coverage will be provided by ABC. Miami is ranked number seven in the College Football Playoff Rankings and the 'Canes are currently favored by 13.5 points. Duke is riding a three-game win streak and has outscored their last two opponents 88-5, while Miami is coming off a 31-24 loss at Notre Dame.

Duke and Miami have played one common opponent. Miami defeated North Carolina 38-35, while Duke lost to North Carolina 24-20. Both games were played in Chapel Hill.

This is the third consecutive meeting in the series where the game will be played in Miami. Miami beat Duke 53-31 in 2024, while Duke beat Miami 45-21 in 2022.

Injury Report: There are no unexpected additions to the injury report for Duke. Safety Patrick Smith-Young and offensive lineman Cole Allen underwent surgery, after suffering injuries in the N.C. State game, and are out for the season.

The main injury for Miami is wide receiver Malachi Toney who remains out with an ankle injury. Toney is a Heisman Trophy candidate and one of, if not the, most explosive players in college football.

Offense: Miami's offense is a juggernaut, ranked number one in the ACC in scoring, total and passing offense, and number two in rushing offense. Quarterback Darian Mensah is one of the best quarterbacks in the nation and even without Toney the 'Canes have an abundance of playmakers.

Duke Coach Manny Diaz on Miami's offense without star wide receiver Malachi Toney: "Anytime you play Miami it still comes down to stopping the run, big physical offensive line, backs that fall forward. If you don't stop that everything else becomes much, much more complicated."

Mensah has completed 212 of 276 pass attempts (76.8%) for 2,694 yards and 28 touchdowns against only two interceptions. Mensah has been sacked 11 times for a loss of 64 yards.

Running backs Mark Fletcher, Jr., (107 carries for 514 yards and nine touchdowns), and Javian Mallory. (74 carries for 509 yards and seven touchdowns), spearhead the rushing attack.

Wide receiver Cooper Bracket (37 receptions for 447 yards and six touchdowns), and tight end Elija Lofton (27 receptions for 349 yards and three touchdowns), spearhead the passing attack.

Duke's defense will have two top priorities Saturday afternoon. First and foremost, the Blue Devils must stop the run, and second, they must pressure Mensah. If Mensah has time to stand in the pocket and survey the field, his accuracy will carve up the secondary. The defense is capable of accomplishing both priorities. Duke leads the ACC in scoring, total and rushing defense, and is fourth in passing defense. Duke has sacked the opponent's quarterback 26 times. Coach Diaz is aggressive with the blitz package.

Players to Watch: Darian Mensah, Mark Fletcher, Jr., Cooper Barkate, Javian Mallory

Defense: Miami is ranked number one in the ACC in rushing defense,but number 16 in passing defense. While opponents average only 64.3 rushing yards per game, they pass for 259.3 yards per game.

The Blue Devils pass game has really started to click the past few weeks and it will need to click on Saturday in Miami. Quarterback Walker Eget is becoming more and more comfortable in the offense and with the receivers.

Offensive coordinator Jonathan Brewer: "I can feel the flow of the offense, especially the passing game, something we have been slowly building toward the last three weeks. We've been moving the ball up and down the field, throwing the football, which opens things up for us, especially … the run game, really lightened the box."

Forcing defenses to lighten the box is critical when your running back is the explosive Nate Sheppard who leads the nation in total touchdowns and total points scored, at 21 and 126 respectively. Look for Duke to be creative in getting Sheppard the ball for opportunities to impact the game.

Linebacker Mohamed Toure leads the 'Canes with 56 tackles and defensive lineman Marquise Lightfoot has recorded seven sacks. Miami has intercepted four passes this season and recovered six fumbles. Miami also has a plus five turnover margin, which is tied for 23rd in the nation.

Miami's defense will try to utilize their big defensive line to shut down the run game and make Duke one dimensional, but that is easier planned than accomplished.

Players to Watch: Marquise Lightfoot, Mohamed Toure, Justin Scott

Prediction: The Blue Devils defense steps up with another big performance and controls Miami's run game as Duke pulls off the big upset. Duke 21, Miami 17.

#2772. Kamario Taylor lands $7.5 million deal to return to Mississippi State

One man’s chaos is another man’s ca-ching.

After years of being stuck in a system that prevented them from getting fair (or any) value for their skills, efforts, and/or sacrifices, college football players are finally getting paid. And one of them is setting a new bar.

Via On3.com, Mississippi State quarterback Kamario Taylor is finalizing a $7.5 million deal to return in 2027. With incentives, it could yield as much as $9 million.

With Taylor under center, the Bulldogs started 4-0. Taylor has since suffered a season-ending injury.

Before the injury, Taylor was regarded as one of the favorites to win the Heisman Trophy.

While the development will prompt the pearl-clutchers to try to parlay the situation into more political momentum for the Protect College Sports Act, it’s a reflection of the free market at work. Which is fine for those in positions of wealth and power, when it comes to their own financial position.

When someone else is reaping the benefits of capitalism in a way that complicates the lives of those who have to balance the books (and to pry cash from boosters who support the NIL funds), it becomes a problem.

And so they’ll do whatever they have to do to roll back the gains players have made. Because free enterprise is desirable only when the people in control practice it.

When the workers start getting paid according to their worth, it becomes a crisis.
